--- original brief ---
# Codex verification brief — sentence-level claim check (commit 0c441e0)

You are an independent reviewer. Do NOT edit any tracked file. You may write scratch
files only under `audit_evidence_claims/`. End with a verdict line, exactly one of:

    VERDICT: APPROVE
    VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED

## What changed

`OncoKG.check_groundedness` (src/query.py) checks a *generated* answer against the
evidence retrieved for the question. Before this commit it only checked that names and
numbers appeared somewhere in the evidence, so "AstraZeneca has discontinued
osimertinib", "osimertinib does not act on EGFR" and "osimertinib is safe" passed.

Now, given `linker=self.link_entities`, it also calls `claims.check_claims`
(src/claims.py), which reads the answer sentence by sentence:

1. A stated relation (acts on / evaluates / sponsors) must hold between the same
   entities in an evidence statement of that relation.
2. Negations, comparisons, hedges and "develops/owns/markets" are reported unless the
   sentence is quoting an evidence statement about the same entities.
3. "phase N" and "N trials" must be stated for that sentence's own entities (a named
   trial is the most specific subject).
4. Every other content word must be grammar (FUNCTION list) or appear in an evidence
   statement about the sentence's entities.

Also: `link_entities` no longer joins several short words into a one-word drug
abbreviation ("is a" was read as "isa" = isatuximab).

The goal set by the owner: ordinary-word claims ("safe", "discontinued") and relations
must be caught. The design is deliberately strict: a true fact the graph does not hold
is reported as unverifiable, and a human reviewer decides.

## What to verify (reproduce everything; cite file:line and exact inputs)

A. **False negatives (most important).** Find answers containing a claim the retrieved
   evidence does not support that still return `grounded=True`. Use the real graph:
   `python src/app.py` exposes `Engine().check(question, answer)`; or call
   `OncoKG().check_groundedness(answer, evidence, entities, True, vocab, linker=kg.link_entities)`.
   Try at least: reversed relations, wrong drug–target / company–drug / trial–drug
   pairing in multi-entity sentences, pronouns ("it", "this drug"), relations stated
   with nouns ("an EGFR inhibitor", "AstraZeneca's osimertinib"), status/phase of the
   wrong trial, counts, negation spelled differently ("isn't", "no longer",
   "un-", "non-"), clinical claims made only of words that happen to be in evidence
   statements, claims split across sentences, lists, Korean or mixed text, markdown.
B. **False positives.** Faithful answers an LLM would plausibly write from the evidence
   (paraphrases, "is an inhibitor of", "is being tested in", lists) that are flagged.
   Strict is intended, but a check that flags most faithful answers is not useful —
   say how often it happens on realistic paraphrases of each demo question in
   `src/query.py:main`.
C. **Correctness of the code**: regexes, sentence splitting, the subject-inheritance
   rule for fragments of one line, `quoted()` bigram rule (can it be abused to launder a
   negation?), dedup in `check_groundedness` (one finding per value — can it hide a
   finding?), the `link_entities` change (does it break any multi-word drug name the
   pipeline should still link? check against the dictionary).
D. **Regressions**: `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (79 expected), and
   that the template answers for the demo questions, read as prose, still pass.
E. **Documentation honesty**: README.md / README.ko.md item 13, the docstrings in
   claims.py and query.py, web/template.html section 06, web/try.html box 3 — do they
   claim more than the code does?

## Report format

`BLOCKERS` (claims that pass and should not, or wrong code), `SHOULD FIX`, `NOTES`,
each with a reproducible input and the observed output. Then the verdict line.

---
name: review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes ??Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** ??does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** ??does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you ??run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point ??a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here ??not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) ??fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3.
- The brief: "Report ??per file/hunk where relevant ??every place the diff violates a documented standard. Cite the standard (file + the rule). Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings ??the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes ??that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing ??**Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions ??**Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

## BLOCKERS

Verified commit `0c441e015117fd9384fa54419ca484fb748dc70b` against the real local graph. **Unsupported claims still return `grounded=True`.** No tracked files were changed.

[Full report with exact inputs, outputs and evidence](<C:/Users/geoum/Dropbox/GY's Formal Workspace/2026 Job recruting/바이오리서치에이아이/oncokg/audit_evidence_claims/independent_verification_report.txt>) · [Reproduction script](<C:/Users/geoum/Dropbox/GY's Formal Workspace/2026 Job recruting/바이오리서치에이아이/oncokg/audit_evidence_claims/independent_reproduce.ps1>)

Question identifiers below denote these exact inputs:

| ID | Question |
|---|---|
| Q1 | `Which companies are developing osimertinib?` |
| Q2 | `What trials evaluate Tagrisso?` |
| Q3 | `What does osimertinib target?` |
| Q4 | `Which drugs act on ALK?` |
| Q5 | `What is AstraZeneca running in NSCLC?` |
| Q6 | `What is the capital of France?` |

Unless stated otherwise, reproductions call `Engine().check(question, answer)`. **Every answer in this BLOCKERS section returned `grounded=True`, `unsupported_claims=[]`.**

1. **Relations are checked without sentence direction or required endpoints.**  
   `src/claims.py:170`, `src/claims.py:183`, `src/claims.py:225` assign roles by entity type, permit empty pair loops, and exempt relation words from further checking.

   | Question | Exact answer |
   |---|---|
   | Q3 | `EGFR acts on osimertinib.` |
   | Q3 | `EGFR is an osimertinib inhibitor.` |
   | Q2 | `osimertinib evaluates NCT03909334.` |
   | Q5 | `osimertinib sponsors AstraZeneca.` |
   | Q3 | `Osimertinib sponsors EGFR.` |

   The retrieved edges support the opposite direction in the first four examples; none supports the fifth relation.

2. **Pronouns can lose their subject when the next sentence names an object.**  
   `src/claims.py:129` replaces inherited entities whenever *any* entity is found. Missing relation endpoints then pass unchecked.

   - Q3: `osimertinib acts on EGFR. It inhibits ALK.`
   - Q3: `osimertinib acts on EGFR. This drug inhibits ALK.`
   - Q2: `NCT03909334 evaluates osimertinib. It evaluates osimertinib and is phase 3.`

   The ALK claim passes both on one line and with a newline before `It`. The trial is **PHASE2**; mentioning osimertinib in the second sentence replaces the trial antecedent and admits other osimertinib trials’ phases.

   Controls: `osimertinib inhibits ALK.` fails, and `NCT03909334 evaluates osimertinib. It is phase 3.` fails.

3. **Evidence scope pools different entities, allowing phases, statuses and counts to migrate.**  
   `src/claims.py:138` selects statements mentioning **any** sentence entity; `src/claims.py:208` and `src/claims.py:218` accept a matching value anywhere in that pool.

   | Question | Exact answer | Retrieved evidence |
   |---|---|---|
   | Q2 | `NCT03909334 is phase 3 and NCT07680790 is phase 2.` | Phases are respectively **2** and **3**. |
   | Q2 | `NCT03909334 and NCT07680790 are recruiting.` | First is **ACTIVE_NOT_RECRUITING**; second is RECRUITING. |
   | Q5 | `AstraZeneca sponsors 10 NSCLC trials evaluating osimertinib.` | Osimertinib count is **14**; **10** belongs to carboplatin. |
   | Q4 | `PLB1003 acts on ALK with 13 NSCLC trials.` | PLB1003 count is **0**; **13** belongs to lorlatinib. |
   | Q4 | `PLB1003 is approved for all lung cancer and acts on ALK.` | PLB1003 has **PHASE_1**; APPROVAL comes from other ALK drugs. |

4. **`quoted()` launders development and negation claims through matching bigrams.**  
   `src/claims.py:148`, `src/claims.py:155`, `src/claims.py:201`.

   Q1 answers:

   - `AstraZeneca is developing the drug osimertinib.`
   - `AstraZeneca has evidence of developing osimertinib.`
   - `This is not evidence that AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib.`

   The evidence says **“a sponsorship, not evidence of developing the drug.”** Matching `developing the`, `of developing`, or `not evidence` exempts the changed claim without checking its meaning or negation scope.

5. **Hyphenated `non-` negation disappears.**  
   `non` is in `FUNCTION` at `src/claims.py:70`; tokenization at `src/claims.py:102` separates it from the following word.

   - Q3: `Osimertinib is a non-inhibitor of EGFR.`
   - Q2: `NCT07680790 is non-recruiting.`

   Evidence states EGFR inhibition and **RECRUITING**, respectively.

6. **Markdown brackets and non-English text can bypass claim checking entirely.**  
   `src/claims.py:87` removes arbitrary bracket contents; `src/claims.py:102` and `src/claims.py:111` only recognize ASCII words.

   All with Q3:

   - `Osimertinib [is safe and discontinued] acts on EGFR.`
   - `[osimertinib is safe](osimertinib) acts on EGFR.`
   - `오시머티닙은 안전하며 부작용이 없다.`
   - `osimertinib 은 EGFR 을 억제하지 않는다.`

   These are skipped claims, not merely difficult semantic paraphrases.

7. **Allowed words and relation synonyms still permit unsupported clinical claims.**

   - Q3: `Osimertinib is a treatment for all cancer.` passes because the clinical assertion consists of `FUNCTION` words (`src/claims.py:65`, `src/claims.py:225`). The evidence only states EGFR inhibition.
   - Question: `What does omaveloxolone target?`  
     Answer: `Omaveloxolone inhibits NFE2L2.`  
     Evidence: `OMAVELOXOLONE acts on NFE2L2 (activator)`.

   The second example exposes loss of action type: inhibition and other relation words map to generic `ACTS_ON` at `src/claims.py:35`; `src/claims.py:185` does not verify the stated action.

8. **Numeric regexes accept partial phases and miss ordinary count layouts.**

   | Question | Exact answer | Failure |
   |---|---|---|
   | Q4 | `PLB1003 is phase 13.` | Phase regex reads only **phase 1**; global number check finds 13 elsewhere. |
   | Q2 | `NCT07680790 is phase 3b.` | Regex validates **phase 3**; one-letter suffix is ignored. |
   | Q4 | `PLB1003 has 13 non small cell lung cancer trials.` | Count regex allows at most two intervening words. |
   | Q4 | `"- PLB1003: NSCLC trials: 13\n- lorlatinib: NSCLC trials: 0"` | Number-after-label layout bypasses count checking. |

   References: `src/claims.py:89`, `src/claims.py:206`, `src/claims.py:213`; global numeric handling at `src/query.py:496`. Actual PLB1003 and lorlatinib counts are **0** and **13**.

## SHOULD FIX

**Faithful paraphrases are rejected frequently.** Six hand-authored answers per demo question produced:

| Question | Faithful answers flagged |
|---|---:|
| Q1 | 2/6 |
| Q2 | 2/6 |
| Q3 | 1/6 |
| Q4 | 3/6 |
| Q5 | 2/6 |
| Q6: appropriate refusals | 6/6 |

That is **10/30 (33.3%)** for evidence-based answers, with refusals reported separately. This diagnostic corpus does **not** establish that most realistic answers fail, nor estimate a production LLM error rate.

Representative exact reproductions:

| Question | Answer | Observed output |
|---|---|---|
| Q1 | `AstraZeneca leads 14 trials investigating osimertinib.` | `grounded=False`; `wording: leads` |
| Q2 | `NCT03909334 is a phase 2 trial evaluating osimertinib and is active, not recruiting.` | False; `wording: active, recruiting` |
| Q2 | `NCT07680790 is a phase 3 trial evaluating Tagrisso.` | False; `drug_name: Tagrisso` |
| Q3 | `"Reported mechanism:\n- Osimertinib: inhibition of EGFR."` | False; `wording: inhibition` |
| Q4 | `CONTELTINIB acts on ALK with a maximum stage of phase 1/2.` | False; `number: 2`, `phase: phase 1/2` |
| Q4 | `"Agents acting on ALK:\n- LORLATINIB\n- CRIZOTINIB\n- ALECTINIB"` | False; `wording: alk` |
| Q6 | `This graph cannot answer that question.` | False; `negation: cannot`, `wording: answer, question` |

Causes include underscore-preserving status tokens (`src/claims.py:102`), missing lexical equivalents (`src/claims.py:227`), literal alias comparison (`src/query.py:529`), compound-phase normalization (`src/claims.py:208`), and colon-sensitive target linking (`src/query.py:120`, `src/query.py:171`). The Tagrisso rejection is inherited from the existing overlap check.

**Correct paired clauses are rejected by the Cartesian-product relation check.**  
Using the union of unmodified evidence retrieved for `What does osimertinib target?` and `What does crizotinib target?`:

- Answer: `osimertinib acts on EGFR and crizotinib acts on ALK.`
- Output: `grounded=False`, with invented obligations `CRIZOTINIB — acts on — EGFR` and `OSIMERTINIB — acts on — ALK`.
- Splitting the same clauses into two sentences returns `grounded=True`.

The same behavior was reproduced for correct trial–drug and company–drug pairs. Cause: `src/claims.py:183`.

**Dedup hides subsequent claim contexts.**  
Q3, answer `Osimertinib is safe. EGFR is safe.` produces two raw `safe` findings, but `src/query.py:545` retains only the first sentence’s finding. Final output remains `grounded=False`; dedup alone cannot turn a nonempty finding list into a pass.

**Documentation promises exceed the implementation.**

- `README.md:508`, `README.ko.md:354`: categorical relation/content-word guarantees omit the demonstrated bypasses.
- `src/claims.py:27`: “negation words are always reported” directly contradicts the quotation exception and observed results.
- `src/query.py:444`, `src/query.py:458`: verification guarantees are stronger than the actual checks.
- `web/template.html:263`, `web/try.html:69`: claims about catching unsupported relations and clinical statements need qualification.
- `web/try.html:140`: the success message says every sentence’s relations and wording match the evidence—even for the passing counterexamples above.

The existing “words, not meaning” caveat does not adequately describe discarded text, unchecked endpoints, or transferred values.

## NOTES

- **Regression suite:** `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` → **79 passed in 5.96s**.
- **Templates:** all **5/5 evidence template bodies** pass when explicitly checked as generated prose, after removing `Scope:` exactly as `src/query.py:389` does. Full templates including Scope fail on metadata counts and `only`. The France refusal fails generated-prose checking; normal `ask()` accepts it through a separate refusal path.
- **Linker change:** compared **2,634 unique stored multiword aliases**, from **7,610 dictionary keys / 1,066 drugs**, against the previous behavior. **No intended links were lost.** `is a` changes from ISATUXIMAB to no match. `AZD 9291`, `osimertinib mesylate`, `trastuzumab deruxtecan`, and `pemetrexed disodium` retain their links.
- **Successful controls:** ordinary `safe`, `discontinued`, `not`, `isn't`, `isn’t`, `no longer`, and tested `un-` forms are flagged. `Osimertinib is an inhibitor of EGFR.` and `Osimertinib is being tested in NCT03909334.` pass. Explicit wrong named drug–target, trial–drug and company–drug pairs are rejected. `AstraZeneca's osimertinib.` is flagged as wording.
- **Execution-scope exception:** the first pytest run inherited system TEMP/TMP; test `TemporaryDirectory()` contexts created and automatically removed files outside the permitted scratch directory. I reran the exact command with TEMP/TMP confined to `audit_evidence_claims/`; all 79 tests passed. Bytecode and pytest cache writes were disabled. Final tracked-file diff is empty.

# Independent claim-check audit, round 2

Reviewed HEAD **a12762b**, against the real local graph, with diff `git diff 0c441e0...HEAD`. No tracked files were edited. All new scratch files are under `audit_evidence_claims/r2/`; the pre-existing `brief.md` and `log.txt` were left alone.

## BLOCKERS

**Unsupported claims still return `grounded=True`, `applicable=True`, `unsupported_claims=[]`.** Every attack below was executed; these are not hypothetical parser concerns. Except the explicitly identified union-evidence cases, they use `Engine().check(question, answer)` or the equivalent direct `check_groundedness` call with that question's unmodified retrieved evidence. Truth here means support in the retrieved evidence, not an external medical judgment.

Question keys are exact inputs:

| Key | Question |
|---|---|
| Q1 | `Which companies are developing osimertinib?` |
| Q2 | `What trials evaluate Tagrisso?` |
| Q3 | `What does osimertinib target?` |
| Q4 | `Which drugs act on ALK?` |
| Q5 | `What is AstraZeneca running in NSCLC?` |
| Q6 | `What is the capital of France?` |
| QC | `What does crizotinib target?` |
| QF | `What does fulvestrant target?` |

### B1. The original reversed noun relation is NOT FIXED; passive detection also reverses active claims

| Question | Exact answer | Observed |
|---|---|---|
| Q3 | `EGFR is an osimertinib inhibitor.` | True; `[]` |
| Q3 | `EGFR is what inhibited osimertinib.` | True; `[]` |
| Q2 | `Osimertinib is what evaluated NCT03909334.` | True; `[]` |
| Q5 | `Osimertinib is what sponsored AstraZeneca.` | True; `[]` |

The sole Q3 evidence is `OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)`, the opposite direction. Q2 says the trial evaluates the drug; Q5 says the company sponsors trials of the drug.

The noun fallback at `src/claims.py:407-411` fills an absent endpoint using entity type after `_group` selects the nearest entity (`:382-383`), reconstructing the supported inverse instead of checking the written subject. The passive heuristic at `:401-404` treats any `BE` token within three words as an auxiliary. In `is what inhibited`, `inhibited` is an active past-tense verb in a cleft, but the checker reverses it.

The first answer is **an exact R1 reproduction**, not a new variant. It is absent from the regression attack list at `tests/test_claims.py:81-122`, despite the repair table's statement that all five R1 direction examples were repaired. Sources: `replay_main_results.json` (`reverse_noun`), `spec_probe_results.json` (`passive_cleft`, `passive_cleft_trial`, `passive_cleft_sponsor`).

### B2. A repeated preposition or an adverb hides an additional relation endpoint

All with Q3:

| Exact answer | Observed |
|---|---|
| `Osimertinib acts on EGFR and on ALK.` | True; `[]` |
| `Osimertinib is an inhibitor of EGFR and of ALK.` | True; `[]` |
| `Osimertinib inhibits EGFR and also ALK.` | True; `[]` |
| Control: `Osimertinib acts on EGFR and ALK.` | False; `relation: OSIMERTINIB — acts — ALK` |

No Q3 evidence mentions ALK. `_group` stops at `on`, `of`, or `also` (`src/claims.py:342-357`). `_clauses` does not split an elided predicate fragment (`:324-341`), and the later wording pass exempts all these grammar/relation words (`:552-553`). The extra named entity is left with no relation obligation. This also affects `target of`: with Q3+QC evidence, `EGFR is a target of osimertinib and of crizotinib.` passes although there is no retrieved crizotinib–EGFR edge. Sources: `spec_probe_results.json`, IDs `endpoint_preposition`, `endpoint_noun`, `endpoint_adverb`, `endpoint_control`, `noun_target_second`.

### B3. Pronouns resolve before the preceding clause is processed; verbatim evidence loses trial subjects

**Clause timing.** Use the unmodified union of evidence retrieved for Q3 and QC, with their linked question entities and the normal vocabulary/linker:

```text
Osimertinib acts on EGFR. Crizotinib acts on ALK and it inhibits EGFR.
```

Observed: True; `[]`. The union contains osimertinib–EGFR and crizotinib–ALK, but no crizotinib–EGFR edge. Replacing `and` with `.`, so that the last sentence is `It inhibits EGFR.`, gives False with `relation: CRIZOTINIB — inhibits — EGFR`. Omitting `it` (`... and inhibits EGFR.`) also correctly fails. The typed variant `... while this drug inhibits EGFR.` wrongly passes.

`_tokenise` resolves the entire unit at `src/claims.py:228`, before `_clauses` and the antecedent updates at `:259-261`; resolution at `:299-300` therefore uses the previous unit's osimertinib. The clause reader updates state too late to change the already substituted pronoun. Sources: `spec_probe_results.json`, IDs `pronoun_stale`, `pronoun_stale2`, `pronoun_fresh`, `elided`.

**Verbatim antecedent.** With Q2:

```text
NCT03909334 (PHASE2, ACTIVE_NOT_RECRUITING) evaluates OSIMERTINIB. It is phase 3.
```

Observed: True; `[]`. The named trial is PHASE2. In contrast, `NCT03909334 evaluates osimertinib. It is phase 3.` correctly fails. At `src/claims.py:221-226`, the exact-statement shortcut chooses the first result of `linker(bare)` as subject. The linker does not produce trial entities; NCT handling exists separately at `:274-275`. Thus the copied statement installs OSIMERTINIB as antecedent, admitting phase 3 from other trials of that drug. Sources: `root_design_probe.json`, IDs `verbatim_antecedent_trial`, `paraphrase_trial_wrong_phase_control`.

### B4. Numeric ownership still migrates between drugs and between attribute types

| Question | Exact answer | Observed |
|---|---|---|
| Q5 | `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib in 10 trials with carboplatin.` | True; `[]` |
| Q5 | Control: `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib in 10 trials.` | False; `number: 10` |
| Q5 | Diagnostic: `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib in 14 trials with carboplatin.` | False; `number: 14` |
| Q4 | `PLB1003 has 1 clinical trial.` | True; `[]` |

The retrieved AstraZeneca count is **14 for osimertinib**, **10 for carboplatin**; it does not establish ten trials combining them. `_owner` searches after a number before looking before it (`src/claims.py:460-468`, `:524`). The appended carboplatin captures the count even though the claim is about osimertinib; the incorrect 10 passes and the osimertinib count 14 fails. The 14 example is a diagnostic of assignment, not a claim that the combination is supported.

PLB1003 evidence explicitly says `max stage PHASE_1; 0 NSCLC trial(s) in this graph`. Numeric checking matches any number in a scoped statement (`:524-525`), so phase **1** satisfies a claimed count of **1** despite the count being **0**. This is a specific, reproducible limitation of counting, not merely a distant-entity issue. Sources: `spec_probe_results.json`, IDs `number_drug_other`, `count_after`, `count_vs_14`, `count_morph`.

### B5. Action validation matches `agonist` inside `antagonist`

Question QF; sole evidence: `FULVESTRANT acts on ESR1 (antagonist)`.

- `Fulvestrant activates ESR1.` → True; `[]`.
- `Fulvestrant is an agonist of ESR1.` → True; `[]`.
- Control: `Fulvestrant is an antagonist of ESR1.` → True, correctly.

`ACTION_WORDS` includes `agonist` (`src/claims.py:73-74`); `:454-455` uses substring containment rather than matching the action token/field. Consequently the action described in the evidence satisfies its opposite. Sources: `spec_probe_results.json`, IDs `action_antagonist`, `action_noun_agonist`, `action_control_antagonist`.

### B6. Heading handling discards claims; the bare-item verbatim pass bypasses joined headings

All with Q3 and its sole EGFR-inhibition statement:

| Exact answer (`\n` denotes a newline) | Observed |
|---|---|
| `osimertinib is safe:` | True; `[]` |
| `osimertinib is discontinued:\nOsimertinib acts on EGFR.` | True; `[]` |
| `these drugs are safe:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)` | True; `[]` |
| `these drugs do not act on EGFR:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)` | True; `[]` |
| `EGFR acts on these drugs:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)` | True; `[]` |
| Control: `these drugs are safe:\n- osimertinib` | False; `wording: safe` |

`_units` defers any non-bullet line ending in a colon and never emits it if no bullet follows (`src/claims.py:170-188`). Thus ordinary content words can disappear entirely. When a bullet does follow, joining does retain the heading (`:177-183`), but the shortcut tests **either** the combined unit **or the original bare item** against evidence (`:221`). A verbatim evidence item exempts an unsafe, negated, or reversed heading. These are separate paths; fixing dangling headings alone would leave the bare-item bypass. Sources: `root_design_probe.json`, IDs `discarded_heading`, `discarded_heading_before_prose`, `verbatim_under_unsafe_heading`, `verbatim_under_negated_heading`, `verbatim_under_reversed_heading`, `ordinary_bullet_heading_control`.

### B7. The META exception accepts biomedical claims, including an invented phase with zero evidence

| Question | Exact answer | Observed |
|---|---|---|
| Q6 | `This trial is phase 4.` | True; `[]`; **evidence_count=0** |
| Q2 | `This trial is phase 3.` | True; `[]` |
| Q2 | `This drug is not in trials.` | True; `[]` |
| Q1 | `It has 14 trials.` | True; `[]` |

These are not graph refusals. An unresolved pronoun should be reported, as the docstring explicitly says (`src/claims.py:22-24`). The early pass at `:233-236` permits every `FUNCTION`/negation word and examines only word tokens, ignoring numbers. `phase` and `trials` are grammar words. The global phase check also skips any one-digit phase whenever a linker is present (`src/query.py:504-508`), even if evidence is empty. A missing-subject phase fragment takes a similar early exit (`claims.py:247-254`).

Control: Q3 / `It inhibits EGFR.` correctly produces `no_antecedent: it`; the bug is the overbroad refusal exception, not an intentional general policy to accept unresolved claims. A legitimate `This graph cannot answer that question.` passes. Sources: `root_design_probe.json`, IDs `meta_france_unsupported_phase`, `meta_missing_typed_phase`, `meta_missing_negation`, `meta_missing_count`, `no_antecedent_control`.

### B8. Refused product spans become opaque entities, swallowing qualifiers and negation

All with Q3; all return True; `[]`:

- `osimertinib is liposomal.`
- `osimertinib is a biosimilar.`
- `Osimertinib is not liposomal.`
- `liposomal Tagrisso.`

The retrieved statement establishes only the EGFR action, not these product claims. `src/query.py:128-141` deliberately emits `Refused` spans and includes neighboring words. `src/claims.py:278-288` accepts them as entity placeholders; `:231-240` rejects `Missing` but does not reject `Refused`. The consumed span, including `not`, is no longer checked as wording or negation. In the Tagrisso example, the new alias exception (`query.py:495-499`, `:543-544`) admits the isolated brand token even though its enclosing product was refused. Sources: `standards_probe.json`, cases `refused_liposomal`, `refused_biosimilar`, `refused_negation`, `refused_alias`.

### B9. Citation stripping still accepts arbitrary content after `ORG:`

Q3 / `Osimertinib [ORG:is safe] acts on EGFR.` → True; `[]`.

Q3 / `Osimertinib [ORG:inhibits ALK and] acts on EGFR.` → True; `[]`.

The regex `ORG:[^\]]+` (`src/claims.py:136-137`) treats arbitrary bracket text as a citation, and `_units` deletes it at `:167`. It neither validates a source identifier nor checks membership in retrieved records. The ordinary R1 bracket example is now caught, but adding the citation prefix restores a text-deletion bypass. Sources: `root_design_probe.json`, IDs `citation_embedded_claim`, `citation_embedded_wrong_relation`.

### S2. Documentation and the repair table still promise more than the code establishes

Even intended verbatim and META exceptions contradict “always”.

- `web/template.html:263-265` and `web/try.html:69-71` retain categorical guarantees about unsupported relations/clinical claims. The softer success text at `web/try.html:142` is an improvement, but still says checked relations and values match when B1/B4/B5 demonstrate the contrary.
- `CODEX_CLAIMS_R1_VERDICT.md:9` says the five R1 direction examples were repaired. The exact reversed noun still passes and is missing from `tests/test_claims.py:81-122`. Preserve the actual R1 cases, rather than treating the 33-case regression list as exhaustive.

### Regressions, templates, linker and scope

- **Tests:** `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` → **128 passed**. The brief's 79 is the older suite size. The initial run had a cp949 subprocess-reader warning; the UTF-8 rerun passed all 128 in 6.24s without warnings (`replay_pytest_utf8.txt`). TEMP/TMP stayed under `r2/replay_temp`, with bytecode and pytest cache disabled.
- **Templates:** all **5/5 evidence template bodies** pass generated-prose checking after the same `Scope:` removal as the normal answer path. Full Scope-inclusive templates still fail. The France refusal template fails generated checking (`no_antecedent: it`, `no_subject: no entity question matched`); ordinary `ask()` uses its separate refusal path. All exact template texts/outputs are in `replay_faithful_results.json`.
- **Original linker experiment:** **7,610 dictionary keys / 1,066 drugs / 2,634 unique multiword aliases**, zero lost intended links from the abbreviation gate. `is a` stays unlinked; `AZD 9291`, `osimertinib mesylate`, `trastuzumab deruxtecan`, and `pemetrexed disodium` retain intended links. This replays the original gate-on/gate-off experiment, not an old-commit execution.
- **New punctuation handling:** there is no `link_entities` implementation change between 0c441e0 and a12762b; the new sanitization is in `claims.py:277`. `Osimertinib acts on EGFR!` passes. The question `Which drugs act on ALK!` (likewise `:` or `;`) still links no entity and yields `intent=unsupported`, while `?` works (`query.py:120`). This is an unaddressed question-path limitation, not a new linker regression.
- **Dictionary-to-parser coverage:** among 2,634 stored multiword aliases, 2,566 link an intended drug ID; five of these lose that ID during claim tokenization (two parenthesized chemical aliases and three parent/salt overlap cases). Exact strings and entity traces are in `standards_probe.json`. These counts describe parser discrepancies, not five newly introduced linker regressions.
- **Successful controls:** explicit reversed active relations, all original wrong named pairs, old clinical/negation attacks, Korean/mixed text, correct basic `target of`, correct `EGFR is inhibited by osimertinib`, and simple elided-subject `and inhibits` cases were exercised. Their passes/failures are retained alongside attacks. Fixes demonstrably improved behavior; the blockers remain independently reproducible.
- **Dedup limit:** distinct-sentence contexts are fixed, but Q3 / `Osimertinib is safe and EGFR is safe.` still yields two raw `safe` findings and only one final finding because both clauses retain the same unit text (`query.py:560-562`). The result remains False; dedup cannot turn a nonempty findings list into a pass. See `root_design_probe.json`, ID `dedup_same_unit`.

Reproduce from the repository root with `powershell -NoProfile -File audit_evidence_claims/r2/reproduce_all.ps1`. New-design probes are `root_design_probe.py`, `spec_probe.py`, and `standards_probe.py`; their adjacent JSON files contain exact input, output and retrieved evidence. Final tracked/staged diffs were empty; `git status --short` listed only the pre-existing untracked `audit_evidence_claims/r2/` tree.

# 주장 검사 Codex 1차 — 지적별 판정과 수리 (2026-11-03 KST)

원문 `audit_evidence_claims/r1_report.md` · 재현 스크립트 `audit_evidence_claims/independent_*.py`. 판정: CHANGES REQUIRED.
지적은 모두 이 레포에서 다시 돌려 확인했고 전부 맞았다. 단어 모음 방식으로는 고칠 수 없어 절 단위
주어–관계–목적어 읽기로 다시 썼다(`src/claims.py`).

| # | 지적 | 판정 | 수리 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | 관계 방향·양쪽 대상 미확인 (`EGFR acts on osimertinib` 등 5건) | 맞음 | 관계어 위치 기준 왼쪽·오른쪽 대상 묶음, 수동태 뒤집기, 종류 불일치 = relation_direction, 한쪽 없음 = relation_incomplete |
| 2 | 대명사가 다음 문장에서 주어를 잃음 | 맞음 | 대명사·생략 주어는 앞 절의 주어로, 앞이 없으면 no_antecedent |
| 3 | 같은 문장의 다른 대상 값(단계·상태·수)을 빌려 씀 | 맞음 | 단계는 바로 앞 대상, 숫자는 바로 뒤 대상의 근거로만, 상태·말은 절의 모든 대상을 함께 말하는 근거로만 |
| 4 | quoted() 두 단어 규칙이 부정·개발 주장을 세탁 | 맞음 | 규칙 삭제. 근거 문장 전체를 그대로 옮긴 줄만 통과 |
| 5 | `non-` 부정 사라짐 | 맞음 | 폐암 이름(non-small cell)만 먼저 지우고 `non`은 부정 |
| 6 | 대괄호·한국어로 검사 우회 | 맞음 | 출처 id 대괄호만 지움, 마크다운 링크는 글자만 남김, 영어가 아닌 글은 unreadable |
| 7 | 문법 단어로만 된 임상 주장, 작용 종류 무시 | 맞음 | treatment·all·cancer 등을 문법 목록에서 뺌, inhibit/activate는 근거의 작용 종류와 대조(대상-표적 근거에 작용 종류를 넣음) |
| 8 | phase 13·3b·긴 단위·"trials: 13" | 맞음 | 단계 숫자 전체 비교, 접미사 포함, 숫자는 위치와 무관하게 주인 대상의 근거로 |
| SF | 충실한 바꿔 말하기 10/30 걸림 | 맞음 | 별칭(Tagrisso) 허용, 상태 구문, 목록 제목 합치기, 명사형(target of, inhibitor, sponsor), 수동 `sponsored by` → 1/30 (`supports trials`, 엄격 의도) |
| SF | 짝 절의 데카르트 곱 | 맞음 | 두 쪽 모두 술어가 있으면 and/but에서 절을 나눔 |
| SF | 중복 제거가 다른 문장의 지적을 숨김 | 맞음 | (값, 문장)으로 중복 제거 |
| SF | 문서가 코드보다 많이 약속 | 맞음 | README 13번, docstring, 대시보드 06, 써 보기 3번 문구를 실제 범위로 |

추가로 찾은 것: 질의 연결이 "is a"를 isa(isatuximab)로 읽던 결함(1차 전에 수리), 대명사 "the target of"를 대명사로 잘못 읽음, "EGFR;"처럼 문장부호가 붙은 이름이 연결 안 됨.
시험 128개(1차 공격 33건 전부 grounded=False, 충실한 바꿔 말하기 16건 전부 통과).
# 주장 검사 Codex 2차 — 지적별 판정과 수리 (2026-11-03 KST)

원문 `audit_evidence_claims/r2/report.md`, `r2_report.md` · 재현 `audit_evidence_claims/r2/*.py`. 판정: CHANGES REQUIRED.
모든 지적을 다시 돌려 확인했고 전부 맞았다.

**1차 판정문 정정**: `CODEX_CLAIMS_R1_VERDICT.md`는 1차 방향 예시 5건을 모두 고쳤다고 적었지만
`EGFR is an osimertinib inhibitor.`는 여전히 통과했고 회귀 시험에도 없었다. 이번에 고치고 시험에 넣었다.

| # | 지적 | 판정 | 수리 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| B1 | 명사형 뒤집기, 강조 구문(`is what inhibited`)을 수동태로 읽음 | 맞음 | 행위자 명사(inhibitor 등)는 앞 대상 = 목적어, 그 앞 = 주어로 고정하고 주어를 종류로 채우지 않음. 수동태는 be 바로 뒤(부사만 허용)일 때만, 행위자는 `by`(시험은 `in`)로 이어져야 함 |
| B2 | `and on ALK`, `of ALK`, `also ALK`에서 두 번째 대상이 빠짐 | 맞음 | 대상 묶음에 on/of/to/also를 이음말로, 관계가 있는 절에서 어느 관계에도 묶이지 않은 대상은 `unrelated_entity` |
| B3 | 대명사를 절 처리 전에 풀어 옛 주어를 씀, 그대로 옮긴 시험 줄이 약을 주어로 남김 | 맞음 | 대명사는 절마다 그 순간의 주어로, 질문의 대상이 첫 선행사, 그대로 옮긴 줄은 NCT를 먼저 주어로 |
| B4 | 숫자가 뒤 대상으로 옮겨 감, 단계 숫자를 시험 수로 읽음 | 맞음 | 시험 수는 "N … trial" 꼴이고 앞에 phase가 없어야 하며, 회사 없는 절에서는 후원 쌍 숫자를 쓰지 않음. 남는 대상은 B2 규칙으로 걸림 |
| B5 | antagonist 안의 agonist | 맞음 | 작용 종류는 단어 경계로 대조 |
| B6 | 항목 없는 제목이 버려짐, 그대로 옮긴 항목이 제목을 면제 | 맞음 | 항목 없는 제목은 문장으로 검사, 그대로 옮긴 항목 아래 제목은 항목 주어를 넣어 따로 검사 |
| B7 | 그래프 설명 예외가 의학 주장을 통과 | 맞음 | 예외는 대상·숫자 없고 그래프에 대한 단어만일 때로 좁힘, 동사 없는 절은 앞 주어를 물려받지 않음 |
| B8 | 거절된 제품 구간이 부정·수식어를 삼킴 | 맞음 | 거절 구간은 `distinct_product`로 보고 |
| B9 | `[ORG:아무 글]`이 인용으로 지워짐 | 맞음 | ORG 인용은 공백 없는 식별자만 |
| — | (2차 원문에 없던 탐침) 동사 없는 조각 `; crizotinib on EGFR`, `On EGFR.`, 단계 주인이 표적, 질환 주장 `is for lung cancer` | 맞음 | `fragment`, 단계는 시험·약에만(약은 최대 단계로만), 질환 단어는 "NSCLC 시험/in NSCLC" 꼴에서만 |
| S1 | 반복 별칭 `(Tagrisso)`, `AZD 9291`의 숫자, 대명사 바꿔 말하기 | 맞음 | 연결을 반복해 같은 약의 두 번째 이름도 잡음, 이름 안 숫자는 숫자 검사에서 뺌 |
| S2 | 문서가 코드보다 많이 약속 | 맞음 | README 13번을 "단어 순서를 읽는 규칙, 처음 보는 문장 모양은 잘못 읽을 수 있음"으로, docstring·대시보드·써 보기 문구도 |

그대로 둔 것: `supports trials`(단어 없는 바꿔 말하기 — 엄격 의도), 대명사로 된 거절 문장(실제 질의 경로는 검사 전에 거절함).
시험 175개(1차 공격 33 + 2차 공격 39 전부 grounded=False, 충실한 문장 24개 전부 통과).

기록 주의: 수리 확인 때 2차 탐침(`root_design_probe.py`, `spec_probe.py`)을 다시 돌려 그 옆 JSON(`root_design_probe.json`, `spec_probe_results.json`)이
수리 후 결과로 덮였다. 2차 당시 결과는 `r2_report.md`의 표가 원본이다.
"""Clause-level claim check for a generated answer.

The name-and-number check in query.py catches invented drugs and invented
figures. It cannot catch a false statement built from true tokens:
"AstraZeneca has discontinued osimertinib", "osimertinib does not act on EGFR",
"osimertinib is safe for all patients", "EGFR acts on osimertinib". This module
reads each clause the way a reviewer would: who is it about, what does it say
about them, and which retrieved statement says the same about the same entities?

What a clause must satisfy to pass:

  * Relations (acts on / inhibits / activates, evaluates, sponsors) are read by
    position: the entities before the relation word are its subject, the ones after
    its object, reversed for a passive ("is inhibited by") and for "target of".
    Both sides must be named (or be the subject carried over from the clause before),
    must be the right kinds (a drug acts on a target, a trial evaluates a drug, a
    company sponsors trials of a drug), and an evidence statement of that relation
    must name both. Inhibit/activate must match the edge's action type.
  * In a clause that states a relation, every entity it names must be part of a
    relation that was checked.
  * A phase belongs to the nearest entity before it, a figure to the nearest entity
    after it; the value must appear in evidence naming that entity and the clause's
    subject, and a trial count must appear as a trial count.
  * A status and every other content word must appear in evidence naming every
    entity of the clause.
  * Negations, comparisons, hedges and modal verbs, and claims of developing,
    owning or marketing are reported; so are distinct products the resolver refuses,
    pronouns with nothing to refer to, and text that is not English.

Exceptions, both narrow: a line that is a verbatim copy of a whole evidence
statement passes as it stands (any heading above it is still checked), and a clause
that names no entity and uses only words about the graph itself ("this graph cannot
answer that question") passes as a statement about the graph.

It is strict on purpose: a true fact the graph does not hold is reported as
unverifiable, and a reviewer decides. It reads words and their order, not meaning,
so a sentence it cannot parse into the shapes above can be misread; the known
residue is listed in the README (known defect 13).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import re
import unicodedata

# -- vocabulary --------------------------------------------------------------- #
# relation word -> (relation, required action, voice). Required action: None means
# any edge of that relation; "inhib" / "activ" must match the edge's action type.
_R = {}
for w in ("act acts acting target targets targeting bind binds binding "
          "modulate modulates modulating").split():
    _R[w] = ("ACTS_ON", None, "active")
for w in ("targeted bound modulated").split():
    _R[w] = ("ACTS_ON", None, "passive")
for w in ("inhibit inhibits inhibiting block blocks blocking inhibitor inhibitors "
          "inhibition blocker blockers antagonist antagonists antagonises "
          "antagonizes").split():
    _R[w] = ("ACTS_ON", "inhib", "active")
for w in ("inhibited blocked antagonised antagonized").split():
    _R[w] = ("ACTS_ON", "inhib", "passive")
for w in ("activate activates activating activator activators agonist agonists "
          "stimulates stimulate").split():
    _R[w] = ("ACTS_ON", "activ", "active")
for w in ("activated stimulated").split():
    _R[w] = ("ACTS_ON", "activ", "passive")
for w in ("evaluate evaluates evaluating test tests testing study studies "
          "studying investigate investigates investigating assess assesses "
          "assessing").split():
    _R[w] = ("EVALUATES", None, "active")
for w in ("evaluated tested studied investigated assessed").split():
    _R[w] = ("EVALUATES", None, "passive")
for w in ("sponsor sponsors sponsoring run runs running conduct conducts "
          "conducting lead leads leading").split():
    _R[w] = ("SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF", None, "active")
for w in ("sponsored conducted led").split():
    _R[w] = ("SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF", None, "passive")
RELATION = _R
# nouns naming the one who acts: "an EGFR inhibitor", "an inhibitor of EGFR"
AGENT_NOUNS = {"inhibitor", "inhibitors", "inhibition", "blocker", "blockers",
               "antagonist", "antagonists", "activator", "activators", "agonist",
               "agonists"}
ROLES = {"ACTS_ON": ("Drug", "Target"), "EVALUATES": ("Trial", "Drug"),
         "SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF": ("Company", "Drug")}
ACTION_WORDS = {"inhib": ("inhibitor", "antagonist", "blocker", "negative"),
                "activ": ("activator", "agonist", "positive", "opener")}

NEGATION = {"not", "no", "never", "none", "neither", "nor", "without", "cannot",
            "lack", "lacks", "lacking", "absent", "fail", "fails", "failed",
            "non", "longer", "nothing", "nobody", "nowhere", "hardly", "barely",
            "unlike", "except", "instead", "rather", "stopped", "ceased"}
COMPARISON = {"more", "less", "fewer", "most", "least", "best", "worst", "better",
              "worse", "superior", "inferior", "than", "only", "first", "largest",
              "smallest", "leading", "top", "main", "major", "primary", "sole",
              "exclusively", "mainly", "mostly", "chiefly"}
HEDGE = {"may", "might", "could", "likely", "possibly", "probably", "suggest",
         "suggests", "appears", "appear", "seems", "seem", "expected", "potential",
         "potentially", "promising", "perhaps", "presumably", "reportedly",
         # modality: what a drug can, will or should do is not what an edge records
         "can", "will", "would", "should", "must", "shall"}
DEVELOP = {"develop", "develops", "developing", "developed", "developer",
           "developers", "owns", "own", "owned", "owner", "markets", "market",
           "marketed", "makes", "made", "manufactures", "manufactured", "sells",
           "sold", "commercialises", "commercializes", "originator", "invented"}

# grammar and neutral nouns that make no claim of their own
FUNCTION = set("""
a an the and or of in on at to for by with from as into within across per via
is are was were be been being has have had does do did
it its this that these those there which who whom whose what where when also
such following listed list here namely include includes including included
trial trials study studies clinical phase phases stage stages max maximum
drug drugs agent agents molecule molecules compound compounds substance substances
organisation organisations organization organizations company companies
sponsorship graph evidence record records data according source sources
retrieved known reported shown show shows mechanism mechanisms profile findings
scope edge edges among lists
""".split())
# a clause with no entity, no figure, and only these words is a statement about the
# graph ("this graph cannot answer that question"), not about the world
META_OK = set("""
graph question questions answer answers answered evidence retrieved scope outside
cover covers covered information available found find determine supporting support
i we this that there here it is are be been was can cannot not no n't do does
a an the of for to in from with so and or any
""".split())
ARTICLES = {"a", "an", "the", "its", "their", "his", "her"}
TRIAL_NOUNS = {"trial", "trials", "study", "studies"}
BE = {"is", "are", "was", "were", "be", "been", "being"}
PASSIVE_GAP = {"being", "been", "also", "still", "currently", "now", "already",
               "reportedly", "directly", "selectively"}
GROUP_GLUE = {("P", ","), ("W", "and"), ("W", "or"), ("W", "as"), ("W", "well"),
              ("W", "on"), ("W", "of"), ("W", "to"), ("W", "also"), ("W", "both"),
              ("W", "either")}
PREDICATE = BE | {"has", "have", "had", "does", "do", "did"} | set(RELATION)
PRONOUN = {"it": None, "its": None, "they": None, "them": None, "their": None,
           "this drug": "Drug", "the drug": "Drug", "this agent": "Drug",
           "the agent": "Drug", "this molecule": "Drug", "this trial": "Trial",
           "the trial": "Trial", "this study": "Trial", "the study": "Trial",
           "this target": "Target", "the target": "Target",
           "this company": "Company", "the company": "Company",
           "this organisation": "Company", "this organization": "Company",
           "the sponsor": "Company", "this sponsor": "Company"}
STEM_SUPPORT = {"approved": "approval", "approve": "approval", "approves": "approval",
                "recruits": "recruiting", "recruit": "recruiting",
                "terminates": "terminated", "completes": "completed",
                "complete": "completed"}
STATUS = ["active not recruiting", "not yet recruiting", "enrolling by invitation",
          "recruiting", "completed", "terminated", "withdrawn", "suspended",
          "unknown", "available", "no longer available", "temporarily not available"]
SCOPE_PHRASES = [r"non[\s\-]?small[\s\-]cell lung (?:cancer|carcinoma)s?", r"\bnsclc\b",
                 r"\blung (?:cancer|carcinoma)\b"]

RE_NCT = re.compile(r"\bNCT\d{8}\b", re.I)
# a citation is a bracket holding only record ids; anything else stays text
RE_CITE = re.compile(r"\[\s*(?:(?:NCT\d{8}|CHEMBL\d+|ENSG\d+|PMID:?\s*\d+|ORG:[A-Za-z0-9_.\-]+)"
                     r"\s*[,;]?\s*)+\]", re.I)
RE_MDLINK = re.compile(r"\[([^\]]*)\]\([^)]*\)")
ROMAN = {"i": "1", "ii": "2", "iii": "3", "iv": "4"}
RE_TOKEN = re.compile(r"\x00(\d+)\x00|\d+[a-z]+\b|\d[\d,]*(?:\.\d+)?%?"
                      r"|[a-z]+(?:_[a-z]+)*(?:'[a-z]+)?|n't|[,;:]")


def _flat(s: str) -> str:
    return re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9]+", " ", s.lower().replace("(s)", "s")).strip()


def _squash(s: str) -> str:
    return re.sub(r"[\s_\-]+", "", s.lower())


def _has(label: str, low: str) -> bool:
    """Whole-word containment: "ALK" must not match "alkylating"."""
    return re.search(rf"(?<![a-z0-9]){re.escape(label.lower())}(?![a-z0-9])", low) is not None


def _non_english(s: str) -> bool:
    return any(ord(c) > 127 and unicodedata.category(c).startswith("L") for c in s)


# -- units -------------------------------------------------------------------- #
def _sentences(body: str) -> list[str]:
    out, depth, start = [], 0, 0
    for i, ch in enumerate(body):
        depth += ch == "("
        depth -= ch == ")"
        end = (ch in ".!?" and (i + 1 == len(body) or body[i + 1] == " ")
               and not re.search(r"\b(?:vs|e\.g|i\.e|approx|no)\.?$", body[start:i], re.I))
        if end or (ch == ";" and depth <= 0):
            out.append(body[start:i + 1].strip())
            start = i + 1
    out.append(body[start:].strip())
    return out


def _units(text: str) -> list[dict]:
    """Sentences. A list item under a heading carries the heading with it."""
    text = RE_MDLINK.sub(r"\1", text)
    text = RE_CITE.sub(" ", text)
    out: list[dict] = []
    heading, used = None, False

    def close_heading():
        if heading is not None and not used:      # a heading with no items is prose
            out.extend({"text": s} for s in _sentences(heading))

    for raw in text.splitlines() + [""]:
        line = raw.strip()
        bullet = re.match(r"^(?:[-*•]|\d+[.)])\s+", line)
        body = line[bullet.end():] if bullet else line
        if bullet and heading is not None:
            out.append({"text": body, "heading": heading})
            used = True
            continue
        close_heading()
        heading, used = None, False
        if not line:
            continue
        if body.endswith(":") and not bullet:
            heading = body[:-1]
            continue
        out.extend({"text": s} for s in _sentences(body))
    return [u for u in out if re.search(r"[^\W\d_]", u["text"] + u.get("heading", ""))]


def _join(item: str, heading: str) -> str:
    if not item:
        return heading
    slot = re.search(r"\b(?:these|the following|following)(?:\s+[A-Za-z]+)?", heading, re.I)
    if slot:
        return heading[:slot.start()] + item + heading[slot.end():]
    return f"{item} — {heading}"


# -- the check ---------------------------------------------------------------- #
class _State:
    def __init__(self, question_entities):
        self.by_type: dict[str, dict] = {}
        self.subject: dict | None = None
        for e in question_entities or []:
            if e.get("type") in ("Drug", "Target", "Company"):
                self.by_type.setdefault(e["type"], e)
                self.subject = self.subject or e

    def note(self, ents):
        real = [e for e in ents if e and e.get("type") in ("Drug", "Target", "Company", "Trial")]
        if real:
            self.subject = real[0]
            for e in real:
                self.by_type[e["type"]] = e


def check_claims(text: str, evidence: list, linker, question_entities=None) -> list[dict]:
    """Return one finding per unsupported claim. `linker(text)` -> entities.

    `question_entities` seed what "this drug" / "this target" refer to."""
    statements = [(e.relation, (e.statement or "").lower()) for e in evidence]
    flat_statements = {_flat(s) for _, s in statements}
    findings: list[dict] = []
    st = _State(question_entities)

    for u in _units(text):
        item, heading = u["text"], u.get("heading")
        unit = _join(item.rstrip("."), heading) if heading else item

        def flag(kind, value, why, unit=unit):
            findings.append({"kind": kind, "value": value, "sentence": unit, "why": why})

        if _non_english(unit):
            flag("unreadable", unit[:40], "text in a language the check cannot read is "
                                          "not verified")
            continue
        if _flat(item.rstrip(".")) in flat_statements:
            # a verbatim evidence line says exactly what the evidence says; it still
            # sets the subject for what follows, and its heading is still read
            item_ents = ([{"type": "Trial", "label": m.upper()} for m in RE_NCT.findall(item)]
                         + [e for e in linker(item)
                            if e.get("type") in ("Drug", "Target", "Company")])
            if heading:
                # the item's own subject: its words up to the first verb
                lead = re.split(r"\b(?:is|are|was|were|has|have|acts?|evaluates?|sponsors?)\b",
                                re.sub(r"\([^)]*\)", " ", item), maxsplit=1)[0].strip()
                # an unlinkable name ("Tianjin Medical University ...") fills nothing;
                # the item itself is already verified
                linked = RE_NCT.search(lead) or any(
                    e.get("type") in ("Drug", "Target", "Company") for e in linker(lead))
                fill = lead if linked else ""
                _check_unit(_join(fill, heading), statements, linker, st, flag,
                            lenient=True)
            st.note(item_ents)
            continue
        _check_unit(unit, statements, linker, st, flag)
    return findings


def _check_unit(unit, statements, linker, st, flag, lenient=False):
    toks, ents = _tokenise(unit, linker)
    for clause in _clauses(toks):
        # pronouns take the subject as it stands at this clause, not at the unit start
        for t in clause:
            if t[0] == "ENT" and ents[t[1]]["type"] == "Pronoun":
                p = ents[t[1]]
                ante = st.by_type.get(p["want"]) if p["want"] else st.subject
                ents[t[1]] = dict(ante) if ante else {"type": "Missing", "label": p["label"]}
        c_ents = [ents[t[1]] for t in clause if t[0] == "ENT"]
        words = [t[1] for t in clause if t[0] == "W"]
        has_num = any(t[0] == "N" for t in clause)

        refused = [e for e in c_ents if e["type"] == "Refused"]
        for e in refused:
            flag("distinct_product", e["label"], "names a distinct product (biosimilar, "
                 "other formulation, tracer) the graph does not hold")
        c_ents = [e for e in c_ents if e["type"] != "Refused"]

        gone = [e for e in c_ents if e["type"] == "Missing"]
        if len(gone) == len(c_ents) and not has_num and all(
                w in META_OK or w.endswith("n't") for w in words):
            continue                          # "this graph cannot answer that"
        if gone:
            flag("no_antecedent", gone[0]["label"],
                 "a pronoun with nothing before it to refer to")
            continue

        pred = next((i for i, t in enumerate(clause)
                     if t[0] == "W" and t[1] in PREDICATE), len(clause))
        implicit = None
        if pred < len(clause) and not lenient and not any(
                t[0] == "ENT" and ents[t[1]]["type"] != "Refused" for t in clause[:pred]):
            implicit = st.subject             # "... and is phase 3"
        subjects = c_ents + ([implicit] if implicit and implicit not in c_ents else [])
        if not subjects:
            content = [w for w in words if w not in FUNCTION and w not in BE]
            if content or has_num:
                flag("no_subject", " ".join(content[:6]) or "figure",
                     "this clause names no entity the evidence can be checked against")
            continue
        labels = [e["label"].lower() for e in subjects]
        support = [s for _, s in statements if all(_has(l, s) for l in labels)]
        covered = _check_relations(clause, ents, implicit, statements, flag, lenient)
        if covered is None and pred == len(clause) and not lenient:
            kinds = {e["type"] for e in c_ents}
            first = next((t for t in clause if t[0] != "P"), None)
            if len(kinds) > 1 or (first and first[0] == "W" and first[1] in
                                  ("on", "of", "to", "in", "with", "for", "against", "by")):
                flag("fragment", " ".join(e["label"] for e in c_ents) or first[1],
                     "a clause with no verb: the relation it implies cannot be read")
        if covered is not None and not lenient:
            for t in clause:
                if t[0] == "ENT" and t[1] not in covered and ents[t[1]]["type"] != "Refused":
                    flag("unrelated_entity", ents[t[1]]["label"],
                         "named in a clause that states a relation, but not part of any "
                         "relation that was checked")
        _check_attributes(clause, ents, subjects[0], support, statements, flag)
        st.note(c_ents)


def _tokenise(unit, linker):
    """Entities and pronouns become placeholders."""
    s = unit
    for pat in SCOPE_PHRASES:
        s = re.sub(pat, " nsclc ", s, flags=re.I)
    s = s.replace("(s)", "s").replace("’", "'")
    s = re.sub(r"[()\[\]{}\"*—–]", " ", s)
    ents: list[dict] = []
    spans = []
    for m in RE_NCT.finditer(s):
        spans.append((m.start(), m.end(), {"type": "Trial", "label": m.group(0).upper()}))

    def blanked():
        b = list(s)
        for a, z, _ in spans:
            b[a:z] = " " * (z - a)
        # the linker strips only " ?.," from tokens, so "EGFR;" or "ALK:" would not link
        return re.sub(r"[;:!]", " ", "".join(b))

    # the linker reports each entity once, so "Osimertinib (Tagrisso)" needs a second look
    for _ in range(3):
        new = 0
        for e in linker(blanked()):
            if e.get("type") == "Disease":
                continue
            words = [re.escape(w) for w in (e.get("matched_text") or "").split()]
            if not words:
                continue
            pat = r"(?<![A-Za-z0-9])" + r"[\W_]+".join(words) + r"(?![A-Za-z0-9])"
            for m in re.finditer(pat, s, re.I):
                if not any(a < m.end() and m.start() < b for a, b, _ in spans):
                    spans.append((m.start(), m.end(), e))
                    new += 1
        if not new:
            break
    low = s.lower()
    for phrase, typ in sorted(PRONOUN.items(), key=lambda x: -len(x[0])):
        for m in re.finditer(rf"\b{re.escape(phrase)}\b", low):
            if any(a < m.end() and m.start() < b for a, b, _ in spans):
                continue
            after = low[m.end():]
            if re.match(r"\s+of\b", after) or any(
                    a >= m.end() and not s[m.end():a].strip(" ,") for a, _, _ in spans):
                continue                 # "the target of X", "the drug osimertinib"
            spans.append((m.start(), m.end(), {"type": "Pronoun", "label": phrase,
                                               "want": typ}))
    spans.sort()
    out, last = [], 0
    for a, b, e in spans:
        out.append(s[last:a])
        out.append(f" \x00{len(ents)}\x00 ")
        ents.append(e)
        last = b
    out.append(s[last:])
    toks = []
    for m in RE_TOKEN.finditer("".join(out).lower()):
        if m.group(1) is not None:
            toks.append(("ENT", int(m.group(1))))
        elif m.group(0) in ",;:":
            toks.append(("P", m.group(0)))
        elif m.group(0)[0].isdigit() and not re.fullmatch(r"\d+[a-z]+", m.group(0)):
            toks.append(("N", m.group(0).replace(",", "").rstrip("%")))
        else:
            toks.append(("W", m.group(0)))
    return toks, ents


def _clauses(toks):
    """Split at ; and at and/but/while/whereas when both sides have a predicate."""
    joins = ("and", "but", "while", "whereas")
    cuts = [-1]
    for i, t in enumerate(toks):
        if t == ("P", ";"):
            cuts.append(i)
        elif t[0] == "W" and t[1] in joins:
            left = toks[cuts[-1] + 1:i]
            nxt = next((j for j in range(i + 1, len(toks))
                        if (toks[j][0] == "W" and toks[j][1] in joins)
                        or toks[j] == ("P", ";")), len(toks))
            right = toks[i + 1:nxt]
            if any(x[0] == "W" and x[1] in PREDICATE for x in left) and \
                    any(x[0] == "W" and x[1] in PREDICATE for x in right):
                cuts.append(i)
    cuts.append(len(toks))
    return [toks[a + 1:b] for a, b in zip(cuts, cuts[1:]) if toks[a + 1:b]]


def _group(clause, k, step, skip=()):
    """Positions of the entity run nearest to k in direction `step`."""
    i = k + step
    while 0 <= i < len(clause) and (clause[i][0] != "ENT" or i in skip):
        i += step
    grp = []
    while 0 <= i < len(clause):
        if clause[i][0] == "ENT" and i not in skip:
            grp.append(i)
            i += step
        elif clause[i] in GROUP_GLUE:
            i += step
        elif step > 0 and grp and clause[i - 1] == ("W", "and"):
            # an elided repeat: "... of 14 trials of A and 10 trials of B" -> A, B
            j = i
            while j < len(clause) and clause[j][0] in ("N", "W") and not (
                    clause[j][0] == "W" and clause[j][1] in PREDICATE):
                j += 1
            if j < len(clause) and clause[j][0] == "ENT" and j not in skip:
                i = j
                continue
            break
        else:
            break
    return grp


def _is_passive(clause, k):
    """A participle right after a form of "be" (adverbs allowed), or before "by"."""
    if k + 1 < len(clause) and clause[k + 1] == ("W", "by"):
        return True
    i = k - 1
    while i >= 0 and clause[i][0] == "W" and clause[i][1] in PASSIVE_GAP:
        i -= 1
    return i >= 0 and clause[i][0] == "W" and clause[i][1] in BE


def _check_relations(clause, ents, implicit, statements, flag, lenient):
    """Check every relation in the clause; return the clause positions it covered,
    or None when the clause states no relation."""
    covered: set[int] = set()
    any_rel = False
    for k, t in enumerate(clause):
        if t[0] != "W" or t[1] not in RELATION:
            continue
        if t[1] == "sponsor" and k and clause[k - 1] == ("W", "lead"):
            continue                       # "lead sponsor": one noun, read at "lead"
        any_rel = True
        rel, action, voice = RELATION[t[1]]
        subj_t, obj_t = ROLES[rel]
        prev = clause[k - 1] if k else None
        nxt = clause[k + 1] if k + 1 < len(clause) else None

        if t[1] in AGENT_NOUNS:
            # "X is an inhibitor of Y", "X is a Y inhibitor": X acts, Y is acted on
            if prev and prev[0] == "ENT":
                obj, subj = [k - 1], _group(clause, k - 1, -1)
            else:
                obj = _group(clause, k, +1) if nxt == ("W", "of") else []
                subj = _group(clause, k, -1)
            noun = True
        elif t[1] in ("target", "targets") and nxt == ("W", "of"):
            # "EGFR is the target of osimertinib" / "the target of osimertinib is EGFR"
            subj = _group(clause, k, +1)
            obj = _group(clause, k, -1)
            if not obj and subj:
                j = max(subj) + 1
                while j < len(clause) and clause[j] in GROUP_GLUE:
                    j += 1
                if j < len(clause) and clause[j][0] == "W" and clause[j][1] in BE:
                    obj = _group(clause, j, +1)
            noun = True
        else:
            subj, obj = _group(clause, k, -1), _group(clause, k, +1)
            if voice == "passive" and _is_passive(clause, k):
                if obj:
                    between = {x[1] for x in clause[k + 1:min(obj)] if x[0] == "W"}
                    allowed = {"by"} | ({"in", "at"} if rel == "EVALUATES" else set())
                    if not between & allowed:
                        flag("relation_direction", f"{t[1]} without 'by'",
                             "a passive verb whose agent is not introduced by 'by'; the "
                             "sentence cannot be read in one direction")
                        any_rel = True
                        covered |= {clause[i][1] for i in subj + obj}
                        continue
                subj, obj = obj, subj
            noun = t[1] in ("sponsor", "sponsors") and (
                (prev and prev[0] == "W" and (prev[1] in ARTICLES or prev[1] in TRIAL_NOUNS))
                or nxt == ("W", "of") or nxt is None)
        left = [ents[clause[i][1]] for i in subj]
        right = [ents[clause[i][1]] for i in obj]
        if not left and implicit:
            left = [implicit]
        if noun and left and not right:
            # "AstraZeneca is a trial sponsor" (of the drug named in the clause)
            pool = [i for i, x in enumerate(clause) if x[0] == "ENT" and i not in subj
                    and ents[x[1]]["type"] == obj_t]
            obj, right = pool, [ents[clause[i][1]] for i in pool]
        here = {clause[i][1] for i in subj + obj}

        if rel == "EVALUATES" and not any(e["type"] == "Trial" for e in left + right):
            # no named trial: the subject must be the word "trials" ("trials
            # evaluating X") or the drug the grammatical object ("X is tested in")
            passive = voice == "passive" and _is_passive(clause, k)
            trial_subject = any(x[0] == "W" and x[1] in TRIAL_NOUNS
                                for x in clause[max(0, k - 3):k])
            if not (passive or trial_subject):
                flag("relation_direction", f"{'/'.join(e['label'] for e in left)} {t[1]}",
                     "trials evaluate drugs; this sentence has something else evaluating")
                continue
            drugs = right
            # with "trials" as the subject, the entity before it belongs to another relation
            covered |= ({clause[i][1] for i in obj} if trial_subject and not passive
                        else here)
            if not drugs or any(e["type"] != "Drug" for e in drugs):
                if not lenient:
                    flag("relation_incomplete", t[1], "an evaluation relation whose "
                                                      "object is not a drug")
                continue
            for d in drugs:
                if not any(r in ("EVALUATES", "SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF") and _has(d["label"], s)
                           for r, s in statements):
                    flag("relation", f"{d['label']} evaluated in trials",
                         "no retrieved trial evaluates this drug")
            continue
        covered |= here
        if not left or not right:
            if not lenient:
                flag("relation_incomplete", t[1], "a relation whose two sides could not "
                                                  "both be identified in the sentence")
            continue
        if any(e["type"] != subj_t for e in left) or any(e["type"] != obj_t for e in right):
            flag("relation_direction",
                 f"{'/'.join(e['label'] for e in left)} {t[1]} "
                 f"{'/'.join(e['label'] for e in right)}",
                 f"this relation runs from a {subj_t.lower()} to a {obj_t.lower()}; "
                 "the sentence has it the other way or between the wrong kinds")
            continue
        for x in left:
            for y in right:
                hits = [s for r, s in statements if r == rel and _has(x["label"], s)
                        and _has(y["label"], s)]
                if not hits:
                    flag("relation", f"{x['label']} — {t[1]} — {y['label']}",
                         "no retrieved edge links these two entities this way")
                elif action and not any(re.search(rf"\b{a}\b", s)
                                        for s in hits for a in ACTION_WORDS[action]):
                    flag("action", f"{x['label']} {t[1]} {y['label']}",
                         "the evidence gives a different or no action type")
    return covered if any_rel else None


def _owner(clause, ents, i, prefer, kinds=("Drug", "Target", "Company", "Trial")):
    """The entity a value at position i belongs to: nearest in `prefer` direction first,
    among the kinds of entity that can carry that value."""
    order = [range(i + 1, len(clause)), range(i - 1, -1, -1)]
    if prefer == "before":
        order.reverse()
    for rng in order:
        for j in rng:
            if clause[j][0] == "ENT" and ents[clause[j][1]]["type"] in kinds:
                return ents[clause[j][1]]
    return None


def _check_attributes(clause, ents, subject, support, statements, flag):
    has_company = any(t[0] == "ENT" and ents[t[1]]["type"] == "Company" for t in clause)

    def scoped(i, prefer, count=False, kinds=("Drug", "Target", "Company", "Trial")):
        """Evidence naming the clause subject and the value's own entity. A count of
        trials in a sponsorship statement belongs to the sponsor-drug pair, so it
        does not count for a clause that names no company."""
        own = _owner(clause, ents, i, prefer, kinds) or subject
        names = {own["label"].lower(), subject["label"].lower()}
        rows = [s for r, s in statements if all(_has(n, s) for n in names)
                and not (count and r == "SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF" and not has_company)]
        return own, rows

    words = [t[1] for t in clause if t[0] == "W"]
    joined = " ".join(words)
    sup_tokens: set[str] = set()
    for s in support:
        sup_tokens |= set(re.findall(r"[a-z]+(?:_[a-z]+)*", s))
    sup_flat = " " + " ".join(_flat(s) for s in support) + " "

    # status phrases are one claim, in the registry's spelling
    for status in STATUS:
        if re.search(rf"\b{status}\b", joined):
            bare = sup_flat.replace("active not recruiting", "").replace(
                "not yet recruiting", "")
            if f" {status} " not in sup_flat or (status == "recruiting"
                                                 and " recruiting " not in bare):
                flag("status", status, "no evidence statement about these entities "
                                       "gives that status")
            joined = re.sub(rf"\b{status}\b", " ", joined)
    words = joined.split()

    i = 0
    while i < len(clause):
        t = clause[i]
        if t == ("W", "phase"):
            j = i + 1
            nums = []
            while j < len(clause) and clause[j][0] in ("N", "W") and len(nums) < 2:
                v = clause[j][1]
                if clause[j][0] == "N" or v in ROMAN or re.fullmatch(r"\d+[a-z]+", v):
                    nums.append(ROMAN.get(v, v))
                    j += 1
                elif v in ("or", "to"):
                    j += 1
                else:
                    break
            if nums:
                key = "phase" + "".join(nums)
                # only a trial or a drug has a phase
                owner, rows = scoped(i, "before", kinds=("Trial", "Drug"))
                own = " ".join(_squash(s) for s in rows)
                if owner["type"] == "Trial":
                    ok = re.search(rf"{key}(?![0-9a-z])", own) or all(
                        re.search(rf"phase{n}(?![0-9a-z])", own) for n in nums)
                elif owner["type"] == "Drug":
                    # a drug's phase is its maximum stage, not the phase of a trial of it
                    ok = re.search(rf"stage{key}(?![0-9a-z])", own)
                else:
                    ok = False
                if not ok:
                    flag("phase", "phase " + "/".join(nums), "no evidence statement about "
                         "these entities gives that phase")
                i = j
                continue
        if t[0] == "N":
            n = t[1]
            ahead = [x[1] for x in clause[i + 1:i + 4] if x[0] == "W"]
            counting = any(w in TRIAL_NOUNS for w in ahead)
            own = " ".join(_flat(s) for s in scoped(i, "after", counting)[1]).replace(",", "")
            if counting:
                # a count of trials must be stated as a count of trials, not a phase
                ok = re.search(rf"(?<!phase )(?<![\d.]){re.escape(n)}\s+(?:[a-z]+\s+){{0,2}}"
                               r"trial", own)
            else:
                ok = re.search(rf"(?<![\d.]){re.escape(n)}(?![\d.])", own)
            if not ok:
                flag("number", n, "no evidence statement about these entities gives "
                                  "that figure")
        if t[0] == "W" and re.fullmatch(r"\d+[a-z]+", t[1]):
            flag("number", t[1], "a figure with a suffix the evidence does not use")
        i += 1

    toks_w = [t[1] for t in clause if t[0] in ("W", "N")]
    for j, w in enumerate(toks_w):
        if w == "nsclc":
            nxt = toks_w[j + 1] if j + 1 < len(toks_w) else ""
            prv = toks_w[j - 1] if j else ""
            if not (nxt in TRIAL_NOUNS or nxt in ("drug", "drugs", "agents", "setting")
                    or prv == "in"):
                flag("indication", "lung cancer", "a claim about the disease itself (what a "
                     "drug is for or is); the graph holds trials, not indications")
    words = [w for w in words if w != "nsclc"]

    seen = set()
    for w in words:
        if w in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(w)
        for vocab, kind, why in (
                (NEGATION, "negation", "the graph stores only relations that exist; it "
                                       "cannot confirm an absence or a failure"),
                (COMPARISON, "comparison", "a ranking or comparison is not a relation "
                                           "the graph holds"),
                (HEDGE, "speculation", "a guess about what may be true is not in the "
                                       "evidence"),
                (DEVELOP, "development_claim", "the graph records trial sponsorship, "
                                               "which is not evidence of developing, "
                                               "owning or marketing a drug")):
            if w in vocab or (kind == "negation" and (w.endswith("n't") or
                                                       w.startswith("non_"))):
                flag(kind, w, why)
                break
        else:
            if w in RELATION or w in FUNCTION or w in BE or len(w) < 2:
                continue
            if w in sup_tokens or STEM_SUPPORT.get(w) in sup_tokens:
                continue
            flag("wording", w, "this word makes a claim no evidence statement about "
                               "these entities supports")

235:     def __init__(self, question_entities):
236:         self.by_type: dict[str, dict] = {}
237:         self.subject: dict | None = None

244:         real = [e for e in ents if e and e.get("type") in ("Drug", "Target", "Company", "Trial")]
245:         if real:
246:             self.subject = real[0]

251: def check_claims(text: str, evidence: list, linker, question_entities=None) -> list[dict]:
252:     """Return one finding per unsupported claim. `linker(text)` -> entities.
253: 
254:     `question_entities` seed what "this drug" / "this target" refer to."""
255:     statements = [(e.relation, (e.statement or "").lower()) for e in evidence]
256:     flat_statements = {_flat(s) for _, s in statements}
257:     findings: list[dict] = []
258:     st = _State(question_entities)
259: 
260:     for u in _units(text):
261:         item, heading = u["text"], u.get("heading")
262:         unit = _join(item.rstrip("."), heading) if heading else item
263: 
264:         def flag(kind, value, why, unit=unit):
265:             findings.append({"kind": kind, "value": value, "sentence": unit, "why": why})
266: 
267:         if _non_english(unit):
268:             flag("unreadable", unit[:40], "text in a language the check cannot read is "
269:                                           "not verified")
270:             continue
271:         if _flat(item.rstrip(".")) in flat_statements:
272:             # a verbatim evidence line says exactly what the evidence says; it still
273:             # sets the subject for what follows, and its heading is still read
274:             item_ents = ([{"type": "Trial", "label": m.upper()} for m in RE_NCT.findall(item)]
275:                          + [e for e in linker(item)
276:                             if e.get("type") in ("Drug", "Target", "Company")])
277:             if heading:
278:                 # the item's own subject: its words up to the first verb
279:                 lead = re.split(r"\b(?:is|are|was|were|has|have|acts?|evaluates?|sponsors?)\b",
280:                                 re.sub(r"\([^)]*\)", " ", item), maxsplit=1)[0].strip()
281:                 # an unlinkable name ("Tianjin Medical University ...") fills nothing;
282:                 # the item itself is already verified
283:                 linked = RE_NCT.search(lead) or any(
284:                     e.get("type") in ("Drug", "Target", "Company") for e in linker(lead))
285:                 fill = lead if linked else ""
286:                 _check_unit(_join(fill, heading), statements, linker, st, flag,
287:                             lenient=True)
288:             st.note(item_ents)
289:             continue
290:         _check_unit(unit, statements, linker, st, flag)
291:     return findings
