294: def _check_unit(unit, statements, linker, st, flag, lenient=False):
295:     toks, ents = _tokenise(unit, linker)
296:     for clause in _clauses(toks):
297:         # pronouns take the subject as it stands at this clause, not at the unit start
298:         for t in clause:
299:             if t[0] == "ENT" and ents[t[1]]["type"] == "Pronoun":
300:                 p = ents[t[1]]
301:                 ante = st.by_type.get(p["want"]) if p["want"] else st.subject
302:                 ents[t[1]] = dict(ante) if ante else {"type": "Missing", "label": p["label"]}
303:         c_ents = [ents[t[1]] for t in clause if t[0] == "ENT"]
304:         words = [t[1] for t in clause if t[0] == "W"]
305:         has_num = any(t[0] == "N" for t in clause)
306: 
307:         refused = [e for e in c_ents if e["type"] == "Refused"]
308:         for e in refused:
309:             flag("distinct_product", e["label"], "names a distinct product (biosimilar, "
310:                  "other formulation, tracer) the graph does not hold")
311:         c_ents = [e for e in c_ents if e["type"] != "Refused"]
312: 
313:         gone = [e for e in c_ents if e["type"] == "Missing"]
314:         if len(gone) == len(c_ents) and not has_num and all(
315:                 w in META_OK or w.endswith("n't") for w in words):
316:             continue                          # "this graph cannot answer that"
317:         if gone:
318:             flag("no_antecedent", gone[0]["label"],
319:                  "a pronoun with nothing before it to refer to")
320:             continue
321: 
322:         pred = next((i for i, t in enumerate(clause)
323:                      if t[0] == "W" and t[1] in PREDICATE), len(clause))
324:         implicit = None
325:         if pred < len(clause) and not lenient and not any(
326:                 t[0] == "ENT" and ents[t[1]]["type"] != "Refused" for t in clause[:pred]):
327:             implicit = st.subject             # "... and is phase 3"
328:         subjects = c_ents + ([implicit] if implicit and implicit not in c_ents else [])
329:         if not subjects:
330:             content = [w for w in words if w not in FUNCTION and w not in BE]
331:             if content or has_num:
332:                 flag("no_subject", " ".join(content[:6]) or "figure",
333:                      "this clause names no entity the evidence can be checked against")
334:             continue
335:         labels = [e["label"].lower() for e in subjects]
336:         support = [s for _, s in statements if all(_has(l, s) for l in labels)]
337:         covered = _check_relations(clause, ents, implicit, statements, flag, lenient)
338:         if covered is None and pred == len(clause) and not lenient:
339:             kinds = {e["type"] for e in c_ents}
340:             first = next((t for t in clause if t[0] != "P"), None)
341:             if len(kinds) > 1 or (first and first[0] == "W" and first[1] in
342:                                   ("on", "of", "to", "in", "with", "for", "against", "by")):
343:                 flag("fragment", " ".join(e["label"] for e in c_ents) or first[1],
344:                      "a clause with no verb: the relation it implies cannot be read")
345:         if covered is not None and not lenient:

443:     # -- answer quality ---------------------------------------------------- #
444:     @staticmethod
445:     def check_groundedness(text: str, evidence: list[Evidence],
446:                            entities: list[dict], generated: bool = True,
447:                            vocabulary: set | None = None, linker=None) -> dict:
448:         """Report what in a *generated* answer the retrieved evidence does not support.
449: 
450:         Two things this check is not, both found by an adversarial review and
451:         written down here rather than papered over:
452: 
453:         1. It is meaningless on the template answer. The template is built by
454:            concatenating the evidence statements, so checking it against those
455:            same statements is circular -- it cannot fail, and reporting
456:            `grounded=True` for it would be a test that always passes. When the
457:            answer was rendered rather than generated, the check reports
458:            `applicable=False` and asserts nothing.
459:         2. It is a reader of word order, not of meaning. Without a `linker` it
460:            only checks that capitalised entities, drug-shaped names and numbers
461:            appear somewhere in the retrieved set. With one (the query layer's
462:            entity linker) it also reads each clause into subject, relation and
463:            object (claims.check_claims) and reports relations, values and words
464:            the evidence does not give for those entities. Sentence shapes it was
465:            not written for can still be misread; claims.py and README known
466:            defect 13 list what is known to get through.
467:         """
468:         if not generated:
469:             return {
470:                 "grounded": None,
471:                 "applicable": False,
472:                 "unsupported_claims": [],
473:                 "evidence_count": len(evidence),
474:                 "method": "not applicable",
475:                 "why": ("the answer was rendered directly from the retrieved "
476:                         "evidence, so checking it against that evidence is "
477:                         "circular and would always pass"),
478:             }
479: 
480:         haystack = " ".join(
481:             [e.statement for e in evidence]
482:             + [str(v) for e in evidence for v in e.detail.values()]
483:             + [e.source_record or "" for e in evidence]
484:             + [en["label"] for en in entities]
485:         ).lower()
486:         # Words are tokenised so "47" is not satisfied by NCT02303977. Numbers
487:         # are tokenised separately and whole, decimals included: splitting on
488:         # non-alphanumerics turned evidence "47.5" into the tokens "47" and
489:         # "5", so an invented "47" passed and the exact "47.5" was rejected.
490:         haystack_tokens = set(re.findall(r"[a-z0-9]+", haystack))
491:         haystack_numbers = {n.replace(",", "") for n in RE_NUMBER.findall(haystack)}
492: 
493:         unsupported: list[dict] = []
494: 
495:         def alias_in_evidence(word: str) -> bool:
496:             if linker is None:
497:                 return False
498:             return any(e.get("type") in ("Drug", "Target", "Company")
499:                        and e["label"].lower() in haystack for e in linker(word))
500: 
501:         # "phase 3" is stated as PHASE3 in the evidence, so a phase number is
502:         # matched against that form, not against the bare figures
503:         haystack_flat = re.sub(r"[\s_\-]+", "", haystack)
504:         phase_nums = {n for m in re.finditer(r"\bphase\s*(\d)(?:\s*/\s*(\d))?\b", text, re.I)
505:                       for n in m.groups() if n}
506:         # digits inside a linked name ("AZD 9291") are part of the name, not a figure
507:         number_text = text
508:         if linker is not None:
509:             for e in linker(text):
510:                 mt = e.get("matched_text") or ""
511:                 if mt and e.get("type") != "Refused" and re.search(r"\d", mt):
512:                     number_text = re.sub(r"[\W_]+".join(map(re.escape, mt.split())),
513:                                          " ", number_text, flags=re.I)
514:         for num in set(RE_NUMBER.findall(number_text)):
515:             if num in phase_nums and (linker is not None or f"phase{num}" in haystack_flat):
516:                 continue      # with a linker, claims.py checks the phase per entity
517:             if num.replace(",", "") not in haystack_numbers:
518:                 unsupported.append({"kind": "number", "value": num,
519:                                     "why": "not present in the retrieved evidence"})
520: 
521:         # A figure written in words cannot be matched against the evidence, so
522:         # it is reported as unverifiable rather than allowed through.
523:         for word in set(RE_NUMBER_WORD.findall(text.lower())):
524:             unsupported.append({"kind": "number_in_words", "value": word,
525:                                 "why": "a figure written in words cannot be "
526:                                        "checked against the evidence"})
527: 
528:         capitalised = set(re.findall(r"\b[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9\-]{3,}\b", text))
529:         stop = {"Scope", "Organisations", "Trials", "Profile", "Findings",
530:                 "Agents", "Reported", "This", "The", "None", "NSCLC", "What",
531:                 "There", "These", "That", "Those", "According", "Sponsors"}
532:         for name in capitalised - stop:
533:             if name.lower() not in haystack and not alias_in_evidence(name):
534:                 unsupported.append({"kind": "entity", "value": name,
535:                                     "why": "named in the answer but not in the evidence"})
536: 
537:         # Drug names are usually lowercase in prose ("xyzabumab cures cancer"
538:         # passed the capitalised-only check). Anything shaped like an INN or a
539:         # development code must appear in the evidence too.
540:         for name in set(RE_DRUGLIKE.findall(text)):
541:             if (name.lower() not in haystack_tokens and name.lower() not in haystack
542:                     and not alias_in_evidence(name)):
543:                 unsupported.append({"kind": "drug_like_name", "value": name,
544:                                     "why": "looks like a drug name or code but "
545:                                            "is not in the evidence"})
546: 
547:         # Any word the drug vocabulary knows ("aspirin") is a drug name,
548:         # whatever its shape, and must be in the evidence.
549:         if vocabulary:
550:             for w in set(re.findall(r"[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9\-]{3,}", text)):
551:                 if (T.canonical_key(w) in vocabulary and w.lower() not in haystack
552:                         and not alias_in_evidence(w)):
553:                     unsupported.append({"kind": "drug_name", "value": w,
554:                                         "why": "a known drug name not in the evidence"})
555: 
556:         # Figures in forms the number matcher does not read: scientific
557:         # notation ("1e6") and Unicode fractions or superscripts ("⅔").
558:         for odd in set(RE_ODD_NUMBER.findall(text)):
559:             unsupported.append({"kind": "number_unreadable", "value": odd,
560:                                 "why": "a figure in a form the check cannot match"})
561: 
562:         if linker is not None:
563:             from claims import check_claims
564:             unsupported.extend(check_claims(text, evidence, linker, entities))
565: 
566:         # one finding per value, first reason kept, in a stable order
567:         seen_values: set[str] = set()
568:         key = lambda u: (str(u["value"]), u.get("sentence", ""))
569:         unsupported = [u for u in sorted(unsupported, key=lambda u: (*key(u), u["kind"]))
570:                        if not (key(u) in seen_values or seen_values.add(key(u)))]
571: 
572:         return {
573:             "grounded": not unsupported,
574:             "applicable": True,
575:             "unsupported_claims": unsupported,
576:             "evidence_count": len(evidence),
577:             "method": ("entity and tokenised-numeric overlap against the "
578:                        "retrieved evidence" + ("; sentence claims checked against "
579:                        "edges and entity-scoped wording" if linker is not None
580:                        else "; relations not checked (no linker)")),
581:         }
582: 
583: 
584: RE_ODD_NUMBER = re.compile(r"\d+(?:\.\d+)?[eE][+\-]?\d+|[\u00bc-\u00be\u2150-\u215e\u00b2\u00b3\u00b9\u2070-\u2079]")
585: RE_NUMBER = re.compile(r"(?<![\w.])\d[\d,]{0,8}(?:\.\d+)?(?![\w])")
586: RE_NUMBER_WORD = re.compile(
587:     r"\b(?:zero|one|two|three|four|five|six|seven|eight|nine|ten|eleven|"
588:     r"twelve|thirteen|fourteen|fifteen|sixteen|seventeen|eighteen|nineteen|"

rows=[]
def run(ident,qs,answer):
    if isinstance(qs,str): qs=[qs]
    entities=[]; ev=[]
    for q in qs:
        en=kg.link_entities(q); entities.extend(en)
        ev.extend(kg.retrieve(kg.classify(q,en),en))
    r=kg.check_groundedness(answer,ev,entities,True,set(kg.dic['index']),linker=kg.link_entities)
    rows.append(dict(id=ident,questions=qs,answer=answer,checks=r,evidence=[asdict(x) for x in ev],units=_units(answer)))
    print(ident,r['grounded'],[(f['kind'],f['value']) for f in r['unsupported_claims']])

> README.md:508:13. **The claim check reads word order, not meaning.** Generated answers are read
  README.md:509:    clause by clause into subject, relation and object (`src/claims.py`, rules in
  README.md:510:    its docstring) and compared with the retrieved evidence for those entities. It
  README.md:511:    is a set of patterns, so it is only as good as the sentence shapes it was
  README.md:512:    written for: two adversarial rounds found 33 and then about 40 shapes that got
  README.md:513:    through (reversed nouns, clefts, a second object after "and on", pronouns read
  README.md:514:    too early, a count borrowed from a phase, `agonist` matching inside
  README.md:515:    `antagonist`, headings, bracketed text, refused product names). Each is now a
  README.md:516:    regression test, but a shape nobody has tried yet can still be misread. Known
  README.md:517:    to get through: a clause whose words, entities and figures all come from one
  README.md:518:    evidence statement about those entities but are arranged into a different
  README.md:519:    claim; a drug name outside the vocabulary that does not look like an INN.
  README.md:520:    Known to be over-strict: a true fact the graph does not hold, a paraphrase it
  README.md:521:    has no word for ("supports trials"), and a refusal worded with a pronoun
  README.md:522:    ("so it cannot answer"). Two narrow exceptions are deliberate: a line copied
  README.md:523:    verbatim from an evidence statement passes, and a clause with no entity that
  README.md:524:    only talks about the graph ("this graph cannot answer that") passes.
  README.md:525:14. **Provenance does not record the code version.** Snapshots carry their
  README.md:526:    fetch time, but the graph does not store which revision of the rules built
  README.md:527:    it, so a change that arrives with a new snapshot and a code change together
  README.md:528:    cannot be separated from the database alone; only git history can.
  README.md:529:8. **The query layer answers the nearest question it can.** "Is pembrolizumab
  README.md:530:   safe in patients with autoimmune disease?" returns a drug profile rather than
  README.md:531:   refusing. It refuses only when no entity links at all.
  README.md:532:9. **`MEDI4736` stays ambiguous** although it is unambiguously durvalumab,
  README.md:533:   because the contaminated synonym blocks the preferred-name rule.
  README.md:534:10. **A two-word personal name is indistinguishable from a short company name.**
  README.md:535:    `Xiuning Le` is a person and `Shanghai Henlius` is a company; only an
  README.md:536:    explicit credential (`MD`, `PhD`) is used to separate them, so the 36
  README.md:537:    `Investigator` nodes are the credentialed ones and the rest stay `Company`.
  README.md:538:    An earlier shape-based guess typed Accuray and Daiichi Sankyo as people.
  README.md:539:
  README.md:540:---
  README.md:541:
  README.md:542:## Running it
  README.md:543:
  README.md:544:```bash
  README.md:545:pip install -r requirements.txt
  README.md:546:
"""A local page for trying the pipeline by hand.

    python src/app.py            then open http://localhost:8765

Three boxes: type an intervention string the way a registry would write it and see
how the resolver reads it, or ask a question and see the grounded answer with its
evidence, or paste an answer (say, one a language model wrote) and see what the
groundedness check flags against the evidence for that question. All three call
the functions the pipeline uses (resolve_drug_string, OncoKG.answer,
OncoKG.check_groundedness) on the built artefacts, so what the page shows is what the
pipeline would do. Nothing is written; the language model is never called.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import sqlite3
import sys
import threading
from http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, ThreadingHTTPServer
from pathlib import Path

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
import config as C
import normalize as N
from query import OncoKG

PAGE = C.WEB / "try.html"
PORT = 8765


class Engine:
    def __init__(self):
        self.kg = OncoKG()
        # A browser opens spare connections it may never use, which would stall a
        # single-threaded server; requests are threaded, the engine is not.
        self.kg.conn.close()
        self.kg.conn = sqlite3.connect(C.PROCESSED / "oncokg.sqlite", check_same_thread=False)
        self.kg.conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
        self.lock = threading.Lock()
        self.dic = self.kg.dic
        self.index_keys = self.kg.index_keys
        target_rows, _, _ = N._load("targets")
        self.target_keys = N.target_guard_keys(target_rows, self.dic)

    def resolve(self, text: str) -> dict:
        out = N.resolve_drug_string(text, self.dic, self.index_keys, self.target_keys)
        for c in out["components"]:
            d = self.dic["drugs"].get(c.get("drug_id") or "")
            if d:
                c["drug"] = {k: d.get(k) for k in
                             ("name", "drug_type", "max_phase_overall", "max_phase_in_nsclc")}
                c["drug"]["mechanisms"] = [m.get("mechanismOfAction") if isinstance(m, dict) else m
                                           for m in (d.get("mechanisms") or [])][:3]
                c["drug"]["trials"] = self.kg.conn.execute(
                    "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                    (d["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]
                par = self.dic["drugs"].get(d.get("parent_molecule") or "")
                if par:
                    c["drug"]["parent"] = {"name": par["name"], "trials": self.kg.conn.execute(
                        "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                        (par["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]}
            if c.get("candidates"):
                c["candidate_names"] = [
                    (self.dic["drugs"].get(x) or {}).get("name", x) for x in c["candidates"]]
        return out

    def ask(self, question: str) -> dict:
        return self.kg.answer(question, use_llm=False).to_dict()

    def check(self, question: str, answer: str) -> dict:
        """Check an answer someone else wrote against the evidence for the question."""
        kg = self.kg
        entities = kg.link_entities(question)
        intent = kg.classify(question, entities)
        evidence = kg.retrieve(intent, entities)
        checks = kg.check_groundedness(answer, evidence, entities, generated=True,
                                       vocabulary=set(self.dic["index"]),
                                       linker=kg.link_entities)
        return {"intent": intent, "entities": entities, "checks": checks,
                "evidence": [{"statement": e.statement, "source_record": e.source_record,
                              "source": e.source, "url": e.url} for e in evidence[:8]],
                "evidence_count": len(evidence)}


def make_handler(engine: Engine):
    class Handler(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
        def _send(self, code: int, body: bytes, ctype: str):
            self.send_response(code)
            self.send_header("Content-Type", ctype)
            self.send_header("Content-Length", str(len(body)))
            self.end_headers()
            self.wfile.write(body)

        def do_GET(self):
            if self.path in ("/", "/index.html"):
                self._send(200, PAGE.read_bytes(), "text/html; charset=utf-8")
            else:
                self._send(404, b"not found", "text/plain")

        def do_POST(self):
            try:
                n = int(self.headers.get("Content-Length") or 0)
                body = json.loads(self.rfile.read(min(n, 20000)) or b"{}")
                text = str(body.get("text") or "").strip()[:500]
                if not text:
                    raise ValueError("empty input")
                if self.path not in ("/api/resolve", "/api/ask", "/api/check"):
                    return self._send(404, b"{}", "application/json")
                with engine.lock:
                    if self.path == "/api/resolve":
                        res = engine.resolve(text)
                    elif self.path == "/api/ask":
                        res = engine.ask(text)
                    else:
                        answer = str(body.get("answer") or "").strip()[:4000]
                        if not answer:
                            raise ValueError("empty answer")
                        res = engine.check(text, answer)
                self._send(200, json.dumps(res, ensure_ascii=False).encode("utf-8"),
                           "application/json; charset=utf-8")
            except Exception as e:  # show the reason on the page, not a dead request
                self._send(400, json.dumps({"error": str(e)}, ensure_ascii=False).encode("utf-8"),
                           "application/json; charset=utf-8")

        def log_message(self, *a):
            pass
    return Handler


def main() -> None:
    port = int(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv) > 1 else PORT
    engine = Engine()
    print(f"OncoKG try-it page: http://localhost:{port}", flush=True)
    ThreadingHTTPServer(("127.0.0.1", port), make_handler(engine)).serve_forever()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

현재 코드는 `grounded=True`를 의미 이해의 보증으로 설명하지 않고, README 13번에 남은 한계를 명시하고 있습니다. 이번 판정에서는 그 항목에 이미 공개된 한계와 새 우회를 구분하고 있습니다. 특히 원문 근거를 복사한 목록 아래의 제목은 별도 lenient 경로를 타므로, 일반 문장과 같은 주장이 동일하게 검사되는지 확인 중입니다.

+from pathlib import Path
+sys.dont_write_bytecode = True
+ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
+sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT / 'src'))
+from app import Engine
+from claims import _units, _tokenise, _clauses
+engine = Engine()
+Q1='Which companies are developing osimertinib?'
+Q2='What trials evaluate Tagrisso?'
+Q3='What does osimertinib target?'
+Q4='Which drugs act on ALK?'
+Q5='What is AstraZeneca running in NSCLC?'
+Q6='What is the capital of France?'

+ ('noun_possessive', Q3, "AstraZeneca's osimertinib inhibits EGFR."),

+ ('fragment_without_be', Q4, 'PLB1003, LORLATINIB.'),
+ ('plain_safety', Q3, 'Osimertinib is safe.'),

+rows = []
+for ident,q,a in CASES:
+    result = engine.check(q,a)
+    entities=engine.kg.link_entities(q)

+    rows.append(dict(id=ident, question=q, answer=a, result=result,

+    print(json.dumps(dict(id=ident, grounded=result['checks']['grounded'],
+       findings=[(f['kind'],f['value']) for f in result['checks']['unsupported_claims']])))
+Path(__file__).with_suffix('.json').write_text(json.dumps(rows,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2),encoding='utf-8')

"""Replay R1/R2 probes at HEAD, writing only to this R3 directory."""
import difflib
import hashlib
import json
import os
from pathlib import Path
import subprocess
import sys

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
TEMP = OUT / "replay3_temp"
TEMP.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
env = dict(os.environ, PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE="1", PYTHONUTF8="1",
           PYTHONIOENCODING="utf-8", TEMP=str(TEMP), TMP=str(TEMP))
manifest = []
names = ["main", "adversarial_probe", "adversarial_pairs", "faithful_probe",
         "code_dictionary", "code_probes"]
jobs = [(ROOT / "audit_evidence_claims" / f"independent_{n}.py", f"replay3_{n}.py", "R1")
        for n in names]
jobs += [(ROOT / "audit_evidence_claims/r2" / f"{n}.py", f"replay3_r2_{n}.py", "R2")
         for n in ["root_design_probe", "spec_probe", "standards_probe"]]
for source, filename, round_name in jobs:
    dest = OUT / filename
    original = source.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    if round_name == "R1":
        adapted = original.replace(".parents[1]", ".parents[2]")
        adapted = adapted.replace("audit_evidence_claims/independent_", "audit_evidence_claims/r3/replay3_")
        adapted = adapted.replace("independent_", "replay3_")
    else:
        adapted = original.replace("audit_evidence_claims/r2/", "audit_evidence_claims/r3/replay3_r2_")
        adapted = adapted.replace("with_name('spec_probe_results.json')", "with_name('replay3_r2_spec_probe_results.json')")
        # Only current internal API adaptation; question/answer corpus unchanged.
        adapted = adapted.replace("_tokenise(answer, engine.kg.link_entities, {}, None)",
                                  "_tokenise(answer, engine.kg.link_entities)")
        adapted = adapted.replace("_tokenise(alias, engine.kg.link_entities, {}, None)",
                                  "_tokenise(alias, engine.kg.link_entities)")
    dest.write_text(adapted, encoding="utf-8")
    (OUT / (dest.stem + "_adaptation.diff")).write_text("".join(difflib.unified_diff(
        original.splitlines(True), adapted.splitlines(True), fromfile=str(source.relative_to(ROOT)),
        tofile=str(dest.relative_to(ROOT)))), encoding="utf-8")
    proc = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-B", str(dest)], cwd=ROOT, env=env,
                          capture_output=True, text=True, encoding="utf-8")
    (OUT / (dest.stem + ".stdout.txt")).write_text(proc.stdout, encoding="utf-8")
    (OUT / (dest.stem + ".stderr.txt")).write_text(proc.stderr, encoding="utf-8")
    manifest.append({"round": round_name, "source": str(source.relative_to(ROOT)),
                     "source_sha256": hashlib.sha256(source.read_bytes()).hexdigest(),
                     "copy": str(dest.relative_to(ROOT)), "returncode": proc.returncode})
    print(filename, proc.returncode, flush=True)
proc = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-m", "pytest", "tests", "-q", "-p", "no:cacheprovider"],
                      cwd=ROOT, env=env, capture_output=True, text=True, encoding="utf-8")
(OUT / "replay3_pytest.stdout.txt").write_text(proc.stdout, encoding="utf-8")
(OUT / "replay3_pytest.stderr.txt").write_text(proc.stderr, encoding="utf-8")
manifest.append({"command": "python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider", "returncode": proc.returncode})
(OUT / "replay3_manifest.json").write_text(json.dumps(manifest, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
print(proc.stdout, flush=True)
print(proc.stderr, flush=True)
sys.exit(any(x["returncode"] for x in manifest))
import hashlib
import json
from pathlib import Path
import subprocess

OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = OUT.parents[1]
OLD = OUT.parent

def read(path):
    return json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))

rows = []
sources = [("main_results", "id", "result"), ("adversarial_results", "id", None),
           ("adversarial_pairs_results", "id", None), ("faithful_results", "id", None),
           ("code_probes", "tag", "result")]
for stem, key, wrapper in sources:
    old, new = read(OLD / f"independent_{stem}.json"), read(OUT / f"replay3_{stem}.json")
    if stem == "faithful_results":
        old, new = old["cases"], new["cases"]
    assert len(old) == len(new)
    for a, b in zip(old, new):
        assert a[key] == b[key]
        for field in ("question", "retrieval_questions", "answer"):
            assert a.get(field) == b.get(field), (stem, b[key], field)
        checks = b[wrapper]["checks"] if wrapper else b["checks"]
        rows.append({"round": "R1", "source": stem, "id": b[key],
                     "questions": b.get("retrieval_questions") or [b["question"]],
                     "answer": b["answer"], "checks": checks})

for stem, key, wrapper in [("root_design_probe", "id", "result"),
                           ("spec_probe_results", "id", None),
                           ("standards_probe", "case", None)]:
    old, new = read(OLD / "r2" / f"{stem}.json"), read(OUT / f"replay3_r2_{stem}.json")
    if stem == "standards_probe":
        old, new = old["cases"], new["cases"]
    assert len(old) == len(new)
    for a, b in zip(old, new):
        assert a[key] == b[key]
        for field in ("question", "questions", "answer"):
            assert a.get(field) == b.get(field), (stem, b[key], field)
        checks = b[wrapper]["checks"] if wrapper else b["checks"]
        rows.append({"round": "R2", "source": stem, "id": b[key],
                     "questions": b.get("questions") or [b["question"]],
                     "answer": b["answer"], "checks": checks})

(OUT / "replay3_all_cases.json").write_text(json.dumps(rows, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
byid = {(r["source"], r["id"]):r for r in rows}

# Round 3 numerical ownership and alias review

Read-only review at HEAD `8d4a606`. All fresh execution used `python -B` with `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` and `PYTHONUTF8=1`. The scripts write only their adjacent JSON under `audit_evidence_claims/r3/`. Evidence was obtained from the real `Engine` graph; no evidence was invented or modified.

## BLOCKERS

### N1. A checked second relation restores cross-drug count borrowing

Exact question: `What is AstraZeneca running in NSCLC?`

| Exact answer | Observed `checks` |
|---|---|
| `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib in 10 trials evaluating carboplatin.` | `grounded=True`, `unsupported_claims=[]` |
| `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib in 14 trials evaluating carboplatin.` | `grounded=False`, `number: 14` |
| `AstraZeneca sponsors 10 trials evaluating carboplatin and osimertinib.` | `grounded=True`, `unsupported_claims=[]` |
| `AstraZeneca sponsors 10 trials evaluating osimertinib and carboplatin.` | `grounded=False`, `number: 10` |
| `AstraZeneca sponsors 10 trials of carboplatin and osimertinib.` | `grounded=True`, `unsupported_claims=[]` |

Retrieved statements separately say `Astrazeneca sponsors 14 NSCLC trial(s) evaluating OSIMERTINIB` and `Astrazeneca sponsors 10 NSCLC trial(s) evaluating CARBOPLATIN`. They establish neither ten osimertinib trials nor ten trials evaluating the combination. Reordering the drug names changes approval while preserving the meaning of the coordinated ten-trial claim.

`_owner` always prefers a subsequent entity (`src/claims.py:594-604`), and the numeric scope requires only that owner and the first clause entity (`:610-618`); it never assigns a count to every coordinated object. Relation checking covers each drug separately (`:561-565`, `:580-586`). The new `unrelated_entity` rule (`:345-350`) therefore has nothing left to reject, even though sponsorship of one drug and evaluation of the other have been composed into an unsupported trial-count claim. The first pair is a direct continuation of the R2 B4 mechanism: inserting an evaluation predicate makes the previously unrelated carboplatin covered.

This is **outside README item 13's listed residue** (`README.md:517-519`): these clauses mix entities and figures from *different* evidence statements. No single retrieved statement names both drugs. A generic caveat that new sentence shapes can be misread is not a listing of this specific residue under the approval bar.

Reproductions: `numeric3_probe.json`, IDs `count_swap_tail`, `count_swap_tail_control`, `count_coordinated`, `count_coordinated_reverse`, `count_coordinated_noun`. Each contains exact inputs, full outputs, full retrieved evidence and parser tokens.

## SHOULD FIX

### N2. Phase owner restriction rejects ordinary faithful trial prose

Question: `What trials evaluate Tagrisso?`

* `NCT03909334 evaluates osimertinib in phase 2.` -> False, `phase: phase 2`.
* `Osimertinib is evaluated by NCT03909334 in phase 2.` -> True, `[]`.
* `NCT03909334 is a phase II trial evaluating osimertinib.` -> False, `wording: ii`.
* `NCT04285671 is a Phase I/II trial evaluating osimertinib.` -> False, `wording: ii`.
* `NCT04285671 is a phase 1/2 trial evaluating osimertinib.` -> True, `[]`.

The evidence explicitly gives NCT03909334 PHASE2 and NCT04285671 PHASE1, PHASE2. In the first sentence, the nearest preceding entity is the drug, so `:657-664` insists on a drug maximum stage instead of the named subject trial's phase. Roman values are parsed at `:647-648` but remain in `words` and are rejected by the final wording loop (`:701-726`). These do not show unsupported phases passing; they show a practical cost of the new owner rule.

### N3. Repeated aliases are only partly repaired, including numeric aliases

Question: `What does osimertinib target?`

* `AZD9291 (AZD-9291) acts on EGFR.` -> False, global `number: 9291`.
* `AZD-9291 acts on EGFR.` -> True, `[]`.
* `Osimertinib (Tagrisso, AZD 9291) acts on EGFR.` -> True, `[]`.
* `Osimertinib (Tagrisso, AZD 9291, AZD9291) acts on EGFR.` -> False, clause `number: 9291`, `wording: azd`.

The global number exemption (`src/query.py:508-513`) calls the deduplicating linker once. The first example therefore masks `AZD9291` but leaves the hyphenated alias's 9291, even though `_tokenise` correctly identifies both occurrences as the same drug. Separately, the iterative linker is capped at three rounds (`claims.py:375`), so the fourth distinct spelling remains ordinary tokens. Digit count attacks `AZD 9291 has 9291 trials.` and `AZD 9291 acts on EGFR with 9291 trials.` are correctly rejected; the exemption did not erase standalone figures in these controls.

## NOTES

* The exact elided-repeat intended form passes: `AstraZeneca is the sponsor of 14 NSCLC trials of osimertinib and 10 NSCLC trials of carboplatin and 8 NSCLC trials of gefitinib.` Swapping the first two counts is rejected (`count_group_three`, `count_group_wrong`). This control supports the repair's intended behavior, not its general safety.
* `PLB1003: clinical trials: 1.` and `PLB1003 has NSCLC trials: 1.` still pass. The count detector only looks ahead from a number (`claims.py:673-680`), so a label-before-number layout borrows PHASE_1 despite the actual count being zero. These examples fit the explicitly acknowledged single-statement rearrangement residue and are not independently used to deny approval.
* Named-trial wrong phase and cross-trial phase controls fail as expected. `NCT03909334 evaluates osimertinib in phase 3.`, `NCT03909334 and NCT07680790 are phase 3.`, and the same coordinated clause with phase 2 all fail. No newly demonstrated out-of-residue unsupported phase passed this probe.
* Fresh dictionary-to-tokenizer sweep (`numeric3_dictionary.py/.json`): 7,610 index keys, 1,066 drugs, 2,634 unique stored multiword aliases; 2,566 link an intended drug ID. Five of these lose the intended ID in claim tokenization, the same five recorded in R2: two parenthesized chemical aliases and three longer parent/salt overlap aliases. This is an existing parser discrepancy, not evidence of a new R3 linker regression. Exact aliases and entity traces are in the JSON.

## BLOCKERS

### D-B1. Clinical-trial claims made only from FUNCTION words are not checked

Question: `What does osimertinib target?`. Sole evidence: `OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)`.

Each returns `grounded=True`, `unsupported_claims=[]`:

- `Osimertinib is in clinical trials.` (`docs3_focused_results.json`, focused_01)
- `This drug is in clinical trials.` (focused_02)
- `Osimertinib has clinical trials.` (focused_03)
- `Osimertinib is a clinical trial.` (focused_04)
- `EGFR is a drug.` (focused_07)
- `EGFR is an organisation.` (focused_08)

Question: `What is the capital of France?`, evidence count **0**:

- `There are clinical trials.` -> `grounded=True`, `unsupported_claims=[]` (focused_10).
- Control `The trial is clinical.` -> False, `no_antecedent: the trial` (focused_11).

This is an unsupported clinical-trial existence/status assertion, not a factual assertion that trial evidence could never exist elsewhere. The audit checks the retrieved evidence for this question. It also accepts objectively incorrect entity classifications. `trial`, `clinical`, `drug`, `organisation` and `company` are unconditional FUNCTION words (`src/claims.py:104-116`). A clause with a subject reaches the exempt-word skip (`:721-722`); an entity-free clause containing only these words falls through the no-subject branch without a finding (`:329-334`). The fragment rule does not apply when `is`/`are` is a predicate (`:338`). This needs no obscure syntax, source-like markup or newly invented clinical adjective.

This is **outside README item 13's explicit known residue** (`README.md:517-519`, `README.ko.md:359-360`): the Q3 evidence contains no `clinical`, `trials`, `drug` or `organisation`; Q6 has no evidence statement at all. The broad warning that new sentence shapes may be misread is not the same as listing this known mechanism, under the user's approval bar.

### D-B2. Record-ID-shaped text can still hide a claim (secondary)

Same Q3 and evidence:

- `Osimertinib [ORG:is-safe] acts on EGFR.` -> True, [] (focused_18).
- `Osimertinib [ORG:inhibits-ALK] acts on EGFR.` -> True, [] (focused_19).
- `Osimertinib [ORG:does-not-inhibit] acts on EGFR.` -> True, [] (focused_20).

`RE_CITE` (`src/claims.py:153-155`) accepts arbitrary letters, dots, underscores and hyphens after `ORG:` and `_units` strips them (`:197`). It checks identifier shape, not whether the identifier exists in the retrieved evidence. These exact content strings are not in evidence. This is weaker than D-B1 as a normal-prose example because it resembles a citation; preserve it as an explicit residual ambiguity. Ordinary visible `Osimertinib is safe [ORG:safe].` correctly fails `wording: safe` (focused_17). The exact R2 whitespace example is repaired; this is a new no-whitespace variant, not a claim that the old reproduction still passes.

## SHOULD FIX

### D-S1. Mostly-pass faithful corpus, with ordinary failures

A fresh hand-authored corpus contains six plausible answers per demo question, selected from the actual evidence before execution. It includes ordinary active/passive prose, lists, aliases, paired clauses, scope disclaimers, and refusals. This is a diagnostic sample, not a production LLM error-rate estimate.

| Question | Faithful pass | Flagged |
|---|---:|---:|
| Q1: companies for osimertinib | 5/6 | 1/6 |
| Q2: trials evaluating Tagrisso | 4/6 | 2/6 |
| Q3: osimertinib target | 4/6 | 2/6 |
| Q4: ALK drugs | 6/6 | 0/6 |
| Q5: AstraZeneca portfolio | 5/6 | 1/6 |
| Evidence answers total | **24/30 (80%)** | **6/30 (20%)** |
| Q6: appropriate refusals, separate | 2/6 | 4/6 |

The evidence answers **mostly pass** this sample. Refusals remain over-strict and the code's normal query path has a separate refusal route. Do not describe the six rejected evidence answers as mostly failing.

Notable natural forms:

- Q3 `Osimertinib targets EGFR and inhibits it.` -> False, `relation_direction: OSIMERTINIB inhibits OSIMERTINIB`. An object pronoun is resolved to the previous clause's subject, not EGFR (`src/claims.py:298-302`, `:243-248`). This is a readable faithful answer.
- Q2 `Phase 3 trials evaluating osimertinib include NCT07680790 and NCT02411448.` -> False, unrelated_entity for both trials plus phase 3. Both trials are phase 3 in the evidence. The preposed phase selects the drug's max-stage rule; listed trials are not treated as subjects of evaluation.
- Q2 `The trials include NCT03909334 (phase 2, active not recruiting) and NCT07680790 (phase 3, recruiting).` -> False, `number: 2,`, `number: 3,`, status and phase findings. The global number regex includes a trailing comma (`src/query.py:514-519`); without a split predicate the attribute support also pools both trials.
- Q5 `AstraZeneca sponsors 14 NSCLC trials evaluating osimertinib and 10 NSCLC trials evaluating carboplatin.` -> False, number 10. The natural repeated evaluation clause loses the sponsorship owner. The nearby elided form with `of 14 trials of osimertinib and 10 trials of carboplatin` passes.
- Q3 `The mechanism recorded for osimertinib is inhibition of EGFR.` -> False, wording recorded. This is the explicitly acknowledged unknown-paraphrase strictness, not a new semantic bypass.
- Q1 sponsorship disclaimer `This is evidence of sponsorship, not evidence that it develops the drug.` is flagged for not/develops despite faithfully stating the graph's limitation. Verbatim evidence disclaimer passes by the intentional exception; paraphrase does not.

All 36 exact inputs and findings are tabulated below. Saved full results: `docs3_probe_results.json`.

### D-S2. Documentation now warns correctly, but the explicit residue list and docstring are still incomplete

- `README.md:508-524` / `README.ko.md:354-362` now explicitly call this a word-order pattern checker, acknowledge unseen sentence shapes, name two intentional exceptions, and list residual bypasses and over-strict forms. That is a real improvement; do not repeat the older blanket claim that these versions promise semantic verification.
- The **explicit** known-pass list (`README.md:517-519`) omits D-B1. Its two mechanisms are rearranging one evidence statement and an unknown non-INN drug name. An evidence-free clinical claim, and claims that introduce `clinical trials` into Q3, are neither mechanism. The user's approval bar requires new known passes to be repaired or explicitly listed.
- `src/claims.py:10-27` is headed 'What a clause must satisfy to pass' and claims every other content word has scoped evidence, relations bind every entity, unresolved pronouns are reported, and values belong to their owner. Some exceptions follow (`:29-38`), but FUNCTION words can themselves assert clinical facts (D-B1), outside the two stated narrow exceptions. The language should describe implemented checks and explicit limitations, rather than present these as universal pass invariants.
- `src/query.py:448-466` has appropriately replaced the former 'nothing absent from evidence' guarantee with a procedural description and explicit pattern-parser caveat; its pointer saying README lists known bypasses is incomplete once D-B1 is known.
- `web/template.html:263-265` and `web/try.html:69-72` now add the rule-based caveat. `web/try.html:142-145` says 'nothing flagged' and expressly says this is not proof of correctness. This success text is honest and materially better than the earlier claim that everything matches.
- `CODEX_CLAIMS_R2_VERDICT.md:6-7` explicitly corrects the R1 reversed-noun repair overstatement. Its citation claim ('ORG citation only whitespace-free identifiers') accurately describes the implemented syntax restriction, but does not establish source-ID membership validation.
- No independent repository style/standards file was found. No standalone style violation is asserted. The user requested source-code comparisons with KOA reference folders only when changing code; this audit changes no source code.

## NOTES

Passive-needs-by controls passed the intended direction tests: `EGFR is inhibited osimertinib.`, `EGFR was inhibited with osimertinib.`, cleft `EGFR is what inhibited osimertinib.`, and `Osimertinib was inhibited by EGFR.` all fail. Faithful `EGFR is inhibited by osimertinib.` and `Tagrisso is being evaluated in NCT07680790, a recruiting phase 3 trial.` pass. Nominal `The inhibition of EGFR by osimertinib.` is a faithful fragment but is still rejected, while the plainly reversed nominal is also rejected.

Known-residue distinction: Q4 `Lorlatinib is approved in NSCLC.` and `Lorlatinib is approved in lung cancer.` pass (focused_13, focused_16). These can plausibly be classified as the explicitly documented rearrangement of one evidence statement, because its source already contains APPROVAL and NSCLC. They are not counted as a new blocker here. Conversely Q3 clinical-trial claims contain clinical/trial content absent from its sole statement.

+# Round 3 independent claim-check review
+
+## BLOCKERS
+
+Audited HEAD `8d4a6060c6d48e6d1d20a2d1470238539bb8fcf9`, with repairs in `a12762b` and `9a80fcb`, against the real local graph. **All exact previously reported blocker inputs are now rejected, but new unsupported claims still return `grounded=True`.** No tracked file was edited. Scratch artifacts are confined to `audit_evidence_claims/r3/`; pre-existing `brief.md` and `log.txt` were not edited.
+
+Question shorthand below always denotes these exact strings:
+
+| ID | Question |
+|---|---|
+| Q1 | `Which companies are developing osimertinib?` |
+| Q2 | `What trials evaluate Tagrisso?` |
+| Q3 | `What does osimertinib target?` |
+| Q4 | `Which drugs act on ALK?` |
+| Q5 | `What is AstraZeneca running in NSCLC?` |
+| Q6 | `What is the capital of France?` |
+
+All new blockers use `Engine().check(question, answer)`, without fabricated evidence or a union of questions. `True; []` means `checks.grounded=True`, `checks.unsupported_claims=[]`. Evidence support means support in the **retrieved evidence for that question**, not whether a claim might be true elsewhere. The README's explicitly listed single-statement rearrangement and unknown-name residues are considered separately below.
+
+### B1. Plural pronouns shrink a coordinated subject to one drug
+
+Q4:
+
+| Exact answer | Observed |
+|---|---|
+| `LORLATINIB and PLB1003 act on ALK and they are approved.` | **True; []** |
+| `PLB1003 and LORLATINIB act on ALK and they are approved.` | False; `wording: approved` |
+| `LORLATINIB and PLB1003 act on ALK and PLB1003 is approved.` | False; `wording: approved` |
+
+Retrieved evidence gives LORLATINIB `max stage APPROVAL`, but PLB1003 `max stage PHASE_1`. Both have supported ALK edges. The first answer extends approval to **both** drugs; reversing their order preserves that claim but changes the result.
+
+`_State.note` stores only `real[0]` as subject (`src/claims.py:243-248`). `they` is an untyped pronoun (`:134`) and resolves to that single subject (`:298-302`). The second clause is therefore checked only against LORLATINIB's approval. The same error occurs across sentences when the second sentence starts with lowercase `they`; capitalized `They` happens to be rejected by the global capitalized-name check, not by correct plural resolution.
+
+This is outside README 13's listed residue: approval is transferred across **two drugs and separate evidence statements**, rather than rearranging one statement about the same entities. Reproductions: [root3_probe.json](root3_probe.json), IDs `pronoun_same_clause`, `pronoun_same_clause_swap`, `pronoun_explicit_control`, `pronoun_two_sentences`.
+
+### B2. Covering every entity in a relation does not prevent cross-drug count borrowing
+
+Q5:
+
+| Exact answer | Observed |
+|---|---|
+| `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib in 10 trials evaluating carboplatin.` | **True; []** |
+| `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib in 14 trials evaluating carboplatin.` | False; `number: 14` |
+| `AstraZeneca sponsors 10 trials evaluating carboplatin and osimertinib.` | **True; []** |
+| `AstraZeneca sponsors 10 trials evaluating osimertinib and carboplatin.` | False; `number: 10` |
+| `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib and carboplatin in 10 trials.` | **True; []** |
+
+The evidence separately states **14** AstraZeneca trials evaluating OSIMERTINIB and **10** evaluating CARBOPLATIN. It establishes neither ten osimertinib trials nor ten trials evaluating both drugs. The reordered coordinated answers have the same ten-trial assertion but different results. The 14-trial combination is a diagnostic control, not a claim that the combination is supported.
+
+`_owner` picks one nearest entity (`src/claims.py:594-604`); numeric `scoped` requires only that entity plus the first clause entity (`:610-618`), and the count check uses that scope (`:672-684`). Both drugs can independently satisfy relation checks (`:540-565`, `:580-586`), so `unrelated_entity` (`:345-350`) has nothing to reject. The R2 example with `with carboplatin` is fixed only because carboplatin is uncovered; adding a checked evaluation predicate restores the original numerical problem.
+
+This mixes distinct evidence statements and is outside the explicit single-statement residue. Reproductions: [numeric3_probe.json](numeric3_probe.json), IDs `count_swap_tail`, `count_swap_tail_control`, `count_coordinated`, `count_coordinated_reverse`; [root3_probe.json](root3_probe.json), `count_shared_group`.
+
+### B3. Verbatim list items still weaken heading validation
+
+Q3, sole evidence: `OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)`.
+
+| Exact answer (`\n` is a newline) | Observed |
+|---|---|
+| `these drugs are sponsors of trials:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)` | **True; []** |
+| `these drugs are activators:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)` | **True; []** |
+| `these drugs inhibit EGFR with ALK:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)` | **True; []** |
+| `Osimertinib is a sponsor of trials.` | False; `relation_incomplete: sponsor` |
+| `Osimertinib is an activator.` | False; `relation_incomplete: activator` |
+| `Osimertinib inhibits EGFR with ALK.` | False; `unrelated_entity: ALK` |
+
+The first heading attributes trial sponsorship to the drug; the second calls an inhibitor an activator. Neither assertion is supported by Q3 evidence. The `with ALK` sentence is a secondary attachment diagnostic; the clear sponsorship assertion alone is sufficient.
+
+The copied item fills the heading's subject, then calls `_check_unit(..., lenient=True)` (`src/claims.py:271-287`). Lenient mode disables missing-endpoint findings (`:567-572`), the fragment rule (`:338`), and uncovered-entity findings (`:345`). Without endpoints, action-type validation at `:587-590` is never reached. Thus headings are visited, but do not satisfy the same constraints as ordinary prose. The exact R2 unsafe/negated/reversed headings are fixed; these are new cases.
+
+The new heading contributes `sponsors`, `trials`, or `activators`, absent from the sole evidence statement. This is neither the intentional copied-line exception nor the one-statement rearrangement residue. Reproductions: [root3_probe.json](root3_probe.json), `heading_sponsors_full`, `heading_activators_full`, `heading_with_alk` and corresponding `plain_*` controls.
+
+### B4. Grammar and disease-context exemptions allow ordinary clinical claims without evidence
+
+| Question | Exact answer | Observed |
+|---|---|---|
+| Q3 | `Osimertinib is in clinical trials.` | **True; []** |
+| Q3 | `Osimertinib is in NSCLC trials.` | **True; []** |
+| Q3 | `Osimertinib is an NSCLC drug.` | **True; []** |
+| Q3 | `EGFR is a drug.` | **True; []** |
+| Q6 | `There are clinical trials.` | **True; []; evidence_count=0** |
+
+Q3's sole evidence contains only osimertinib's EGFR inhibition; it contains no clinical-trial or disease assertion. Q6 retrieves nothing. These are substantive claims, not graph refusals.
+
+`clinical`, `trial(s)`, `drug(s)` and entity-type nouns are in `FUNCTION` (`src/claims.py:104-116`). With an entity they bypass wording validation (`:721-722`); without an entity, the no-subject branch reports nothing when all words are grammar (`:329-334`). The `nsclc` rule permits adjacency to `trials`/`drug(s)` or a preceding `in`, then removes the token without requiring disease evidence (`:690-699`). `Osimertinib is for lung cancer.` correctly fails, but changing the construction to `is an NSCLC drug` passes.
+
+Related unchecked copulas show the fragment rule's limit: Q4 / `PLB1003 is LORLATINIB.` and Q2 / `NCT03909334 is NCT07680790.` both pass. `is` counts as a predicate, so the fragment guard (`:338-344`) does not apply, while it is not a checked relation. No retrieved statement equates these separate entities.
+
+The core clinical examples add words absent from Q3 evidence, and Q6 has no statement to rearrange. They fall outside README 13's two explicit known-pass residues. Reproductions: [root3_probe.json](root3_probe.json), `clinical_trial_unsupported`, `nsclc_trial_unsupported`, `indication_drug`, `zero_evidence_clinical`, `fragment_drug_identity`, `fragment_trial_identity`; [docs3_focused_results.json](docs3_focused_results.json), `focused_07` for `EGFR is a drug.`.
+
+## SHOULD FIX
+
+### S1. Faithful evidence answers mostly pass, with natural forms still rejected
+
+The original R1 corpus was replayed unchanged. A separate fresh corpus contains six hand-authored faithful answers per demo question. These are diagnostic samples, not a production LLM error-rate estimate; they should not be pooled as independent statistical samples.
+
+| Question | Original corpus passed | Fresh corpus passed |
+|---|---:|---:|
+| Q1 | 6/6 | 5/6 |
+| Q2 | 6/6 | 4/6 |
+| Q3 | 6/6 | 4/6 |
+| Q4 | 6/6 | 6/6 |
+| Q5 | 5/6 | 5/6 |
+| Evidence answers | **29/30 (96.7%)** | **24/30 (80%)** |
+| Q6 refusals, separate | 4/6 | 2/6 |
+
+Thus the tested evidence answers satisfy the **mostly-pass** part of the bar. The verdict is driven by B1–B4 and the incomplete documented residue, not by a claim that most faithful answers fail.
+
+Representative exact failures:
+
+| Q | Exact faithful answer | Observed |
+|---|---|---|
+| Q3 | `Osimertinib targets EGFR and inhibits it.` | False; `relation_direction: OSIMERTINIB inhibits OSIMERTINIB` |
+| Q2 | `NCT03909334 evaluates osimertinib in phase 2.` | False; `phase: phase 2` |
+| Q2 | `NCT03909334 is a phase II trial evaluating osimertinib.` | False; `wording: ii` |
+| Q5 | `AstraZeneca sponsors 14 NSCLC trials evaluating osimertinib and 10 NSCLC trials evaluating carboplatin.` | False; `number: 10` |
+| Q3 | `AZD9291 (AZD-9291) acts on EGFR.` | False; global `number: 9291` |
+| Q3 | `Osimertinib (Tagrisso, AZD 9291, AZD9291) acts on EGFR.` | False; clause `number: 9291`, `wording: azd` |
+
+Object pronouns are assigned the prior subject (`claims.py:298-302`). A phase after the drug selects drug max-stage evidence even when the sentence's named trial has the stated phase (`:657-664`). Roman phases are parsed (`:647-648`) but remain in the later wording list (`:701-726`). Repeated evaluation clauses split away the company owner (`:420-439`, `:614-617`). The global numeric-name exemption calls the deduplicating linker once (`query.py:508-513`), while claim tokenization permits only three alias-linking rounds (`claims.py:375`), producing separate repeated-alias failures.
+
+`AZD-9291 acts on EGFR.` and `Osimertinib (Tagrisso, AZD 9291) acts on EGFR.` pass. The correct count-elision form `AstraZeneca sponsors 14 trials of osimertinib and 10 trials of carboplatin.` passes. Exact evidence, full findings, all corpus answers and more controls are in [docs3_review.md](docs3_review.md), [docs3_probe_results.json](docs3_probe_results.json), and [numeric3_findings.md](numeric3_findings.md).
+
+### S2. Documentation is substantially more candid, but still does not cover known passes
+
+`README.md:508-524` and `README.ko.md:354-362` now explicitly describe a word-order pattern checker with unseen-shape limitations. `src/query.py:448-466` no longer promises semantic verification. `web/template.html:263-265` and `web/try.html:69-72` include the caveat; `web/try.html:142-145` says nothing was flagged and expressly disclaims proof of correctness. Those are improvements, and the old blanket documentation criticism should not be repeated unchanged.
+
+Nevertheless, the **explicit known-pass list** in `README.md:517-519` covers rearranging one evidence statement and an unknown non-INN drug name. B1–B4 involve multiple statements, skipped heading obligations, new clinical vocabulary, or no evidence. A generic warning that unseen shapes can fail does not specifically list these residues under the requested approval bar.
+
+The `claims.py:10-27` docstring frames its rules as what a clause **must satisfy to pass**: all relation entities covered, both endpoints identified, values scoped, and content supported. B1–B4 show additional exceptions beyond the two named at `:29-33`. Revise the universal phrasing and explicit residue or repair these behaviors before approval. The R2 repair table correctly retracts the old reversed-noun repair claim (`CODEX_CLAIMS_R2_VERDICT.md:6-7`); the exact prior direction cases now fail.
+
+No separate repository coding-standard document was found, and no standalone style violation is asserted. This is a behavioral/specification and documentation review, not a source-code change; the instruction to compare changed source against KOA reference folders is therefore not triggered. Separate documentation/standards reasoning is in [docs3_review.md](docs3_review.md).

+ ('count_cross_pair_control', Q5, 'AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib in 14 trials evaluating carboplatin.'),
+ ('count_shared_group', Q5, 'AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib and carboplatin in 10 trials.'),
+ ('count_shared_group_control', Q5, 'AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib and carboplatin in 14 trials.'),

+ ('clinical_trial_unsupported', Q3, 'Osimertinib is in clinical trials.'),
+ ('faithful_relation_passive', Q3, 'EGFR is inhibited by osimertinib.'),
+]
+rows = []
+for ident,q,a in CASES:
+    result = engine.check(q,a)
+    entities=engine.kg.link_entities(q)
+    ev=engine.kg.retrieve(engine.kg.classify(q,entities),entities)
+    rows.append(dict(id=ident, question=q, answer=a, result=result,
+                     evidence=[vars(x) for x in ev], units=_units(a)))
+    print(json.dumps(dict(id=ident, grounded=result['checks']['grounded'],
+       findings=[(f['kind'],f['value']) for f in result['checks']['unsupported_claims']])))
+Path(__file__).with_suffix('.json').write_text(json.dumps(rows,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2),encoding='utf-8')

+"""Verify saved audit outputs without rerunning the completed tests."""
+import hashlib
+import json
+from pathlib import Path
+import subprocess
+import sys
+sys.dont_write_bytecode = True
+OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
+ROOT = OUT.parents[1]
+def read(name):
+    return json.loads((OUT / name).read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
+def git(*args):
+    p = subprocess.run(['git', *args], cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True, text=True, encoding='utf-8')
+    return dict(returncode=p.returncode, stdout=p.stdout, stderr=p.stderr)
+root = read('root3_probe.json')
+ids = {r['id']: r for r in root}
+blockers = ['pronoun_same_clause', 'count_cross_pair', 'count_shared_group',
+            'heading_sponsors_full', 'heading_activators_full',
+            'clinical_trial_unsupported', 'nsclc_trial_unsupported', 'zero_evidence_clinical']
+for ident in blockers:
+    c = ids[ident]['result']['checks']
+    assert c['grounded'] is True and c['unsupported_claims'] == [], ident
+controls = ['pronoun_same_clause_swap', 'pronoun_explicit_control',
+            'count_cross_pair_control', 'plain_sponsors_full', 'plain_activators_full',
+            'question_seed_bad', 'question_seed_wrong_type']
+for ident in controls:
+    assert ids[ident]['result']['checks']['grounded'] is False, ident
+assert ids['zero_evidence_clinical']['result']['checks']['evidence_count'] == 0
+replay = read('replay3_all_cases.json')
+assert len(replay) == 246
+docs = read('docs3_probe_results.json')
+faithful = [r for r in docs if r['family'] == 'faithful']
+assert sum(r['result']['checks']['grounded'] for r in faithful if r['q'] < 6) == 24
+assert len([r for r in faithful if r['q'] < 6]) == 30
+assert '175 passed' in (OUT / 'replay3_pytest.stdout.txt').read_text(encoding='utf-8')
+tracked = git('diff', '--exit-code', 'HEAD', '--', '.')
+staged = git('diff', '--cached', '--exit-code')
+assert tracked['returncode'] == staged['returncode'] == 0
+source_paths = ['src/claims.py', 'src/query.py', 'src/app.py', 'tests/test_claims.py',
+                'README.md', 'README.ko.md', 'web/template.html', 'web/try.html',
+                'data/processed/oncokg.sqlite']
+hashes = {name:hashlib.sha256((ROOT/name).read_bytes()).hexdigest()
+          for name in source_paths if (ROOT/name).is_file()}
+baseline = read('replay3_verification.json')
+result = dict(head=git('rev-parse', 'HEAD')['stdout'].strip(),
+              tracked_diff=tracked, staged_diff=staged, git_status=git('status', '--short'),
+              source_sha256=hashes, replay_cases=len(replay), root_probe_cases=len(root),
+              numeric_probe_cases=len(read('numeric3_probe.json')),
+              documentation_probe_cases=len(docs), focused_probe_cases=len(read('docs3_focused_results.json')),
+              verified_new_blocker_ids=blockers, verified_negative_control_ids=controls,
+              fresh_faithful_evidence_pass='24/30', fresh_refusal_pass='2/6',
+              pytest=(OUT/'replay3_pytest.stdout.txt').read_text(encoding='utf-8').strip(),
+              earlier_verification=baseline,
+              artifact_sha256={p.name:hashlib.sha256(p.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
+                               for p in OUT.iterdir() if p.is_file()
+                               and p.name not in ('brief.md','log.txt','r3_verification.json')})
+(OUT/'r3_verification.json').write_text(json.dumps(result,indent=2,ensure_ascii=False),encoding='utf-8')
+print(json.dumps({k:result[k] for k in ['head','replay_cases','root_probe_cases','numeric_probe_cases',
+                                      'documentation_probe_cases','focused_probe_cases','pytest']},ensure_ascii=True))

| 질문 | 통과한 답변 | 문제 |
|---|---|---|
| `Which drugs act on ALK?` | `LORLATINIB and PLB1003 act on ALK and they are approved.` | `they`가 첫 약물로 축소됩니다. PLB1003의 근거는 PHASE_1입니다. |
| `What is AstraZeneca running in NSCLC?` | `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib in 10 trials evaluating carboplatin.` | osimertinib의 14건과 carboplatin의 10건을 섞어, 근거 없는 공동 시험 수를 허용합니다. |
| `What does osimertinib target?` | `these drugs are sponsors of trials:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)` | 원문 항목 위 제목은 lenient 검사에서 관계 양쪽 대상 확인이 생략됩니다. |
| `What does osimertinib target?` | `Osimertinib is in clinical trials.` | 검색 근거는 EGFR 억제뿐인데 임상시험 주장이 문법 단어로 면제됩니다. |

이 네 결함군은 README 13번에 명시된 잔여 한계에 해당하지 않습니다.

**SHOULD FIX**
